## Device evidence (owner photo of v1.8, 23:58 CST 2026-11-14)
1. Whole screen renders BLUE (diagonal, bright bottom-right) although the
   shipped gradient was near-black.
2. Pale CYAN blob, larger than the pill, covers the focused TV pill, text

3. Phim home: "Đang tải thư viện phim…" spinner forever, ZERO rails.

## Auditor A findings (visual/focus) — CONFIRMED from code
- JoeTheme.background is the ONLY full-screen painter; its "black" stops are
  blue-biased (B ≈ 1.18× R/G; #1B1B20 itself has B=0x20 > R=G=0x1B), brightest
  bottom-right = the photo's pattern. FIX: solid Color.black (hue-free).
- The blob is NOT app-drawn (pill focus rendering = fill behind label + inner
  stroke + scale 1.06; no shadow; JoeFocusRing unused on pills). It is the
  system focus effect; the root-level .focusEffectDisabled(true) did NOT
  suppress it on the owner's tvOS (Liquid Glass). FIX: per-view modifier on
  every focusable; contingency = non-Button .focusable() chrome.
- JoeTheme.swift header comment (lines 3-11) is stale (retired indigo design).

## Auditor B findings (data/network) — CONFIRMED from code + VM measurement
- Launch = 24 concurrent jobs, up to 58 requests; 17 are KK jobs each with
  SEQUENTIAL 3-host failover × 25 s. NO overall deadline: `loading` clears
  only when the slowest job ends; the error+retry UI is unreachable until
  then. Worst case ~2-4 min of spinner on a hanging route.
- hasData = merged/kkNew only; empty-but-valid responses count as success,
  loadedAt stays unset → every return to the tab re-fires the full storm.
- PhimStore has NO disk cache; the TV section (TVStore) is cache-first,
  single friendly host (GitHub raw), visible failure status — the model to copy.
- All provider endpoints answer from the VM with parser-valid JSON
  (KK A/B, VS, F4K, TP all 200, ~0.7-1.6 s). KK host C (free2.phim4k.dev)
  returns the {"status":false} 404 stub for list calls from the VM — dead leg.
- Errors are discarded by try? everywhere — no failure can ever be diagnosed.
- UNVERIFIED: the ATV's actual Sing-box profile/routing (leading hypothesis
  for zero rails on device); tvOS connect-phase timeout behaviour.

## Store → view contract (both teams build to this exactly)
- PhimStore gains `@Published var loadTimedOut = false`: set true when a
  load has run 20 s with hasData still false; cleared when data lands or a
  new load starts. JoePhimHome shows the error+retry block when
  `phim.loadTimedOut || (!phim.loading && !phim.hasData)`.
- SourceProbe (Team 2) exposes per-provider probe results for Settings;
  Team 1 renders them in JoeSettings.swift via the agreed type:
  `SourceProbe.run() async -> [SourceProbe.Result]` with
  `Result { name: String; ok: Bool; millis: Int; note: String }`.

## Standing owner laws (all teams)
- NO white/pale focus styling anywhere; focus is app-drawn only (violet
  outline/fill on the element's own shape). .buttonStyle(.card) is BANNED.
- Background: hue-free black. Real JOE TV logo in the top bar.
- The two Apple TV apps (Joe TV vs Đá Banh TV) must look clearly different.
- Anything Muse can fill in, the owner never types (TMDB key is bundled
  via build-time secret — already working, do not regress it).
- A movie app whose links don't play is not a movie app: loading must
  always resolve to content, a visible error, or a cached library —
  never an endless spinner.

## ★ NEW DEVICE EVIDENCE — SEVERITY 1 (owner, 2026-11-15 00:08 CST)
"v1.8 làm chính Apple TV freeze. Bấm rất nhiều lần mới thoát được về home."
The app doesn't just spin — it makes the whole Apple TV feel frozen and
fights the user's exit (many Menu presses needed to reach Home).
NEW STANDING REQUIREMENT for every team: the app must NEVER impede exit
or saturate the main thread/focus engine. Prime suspects to prove or clear:
(a) the exit/Menu handling (JoeExitTrap / onExitCommand) consuming Menu
presses at root; (b) a launch focus loop (hub nonce + traps re-assigning
focus cyclically); (c) main-actor blocking (JoeProxy semaphore wait,
parsing/publishing churn); (d) infinite view invalidation. Menu at root =
the app exits immediately, no trap, no dialog. Every focus request must
be idempotent and terminating.

## OWNER DIRECTIVE — SELF-SUBSTITUTING SOURCES (2026-11-15 ~00:40 CST)
Anh's ruling, verbatim sense: the problem is not whether a link is dead;
when a link dies the app must FIND A REPLACEMENT. A proper app is always
ready. In the Phim section, if a title's links are missing/stalled/dead,
the app flexibly pulls the TORRENT link for the SAME title as the
substitute — identity is by the movie's ID. The app must query the other
source itself and produce the replacement link immediately.
Design (leader): the substitution rung joins PlayDirector's ladder as
the FINAL automatic rung. Chain: Phim detail already carries the TMDB
id → TMDB external_ids gives the IMDB id → Torrentio streams keyed by
that IMDB id (series: imdb_id:S:E from the selected episode) → existing
container rule filters unplayables → Real-Debrid unrestrict → same
player. Triggers: (a) every Phim provider fails to resolve/play a title;
(b) mid-play stall via the existing 20 s watchdog exhausting the Phim
rungs. Only if the torrent rung also fails does the exhausted slate
appear. Matching is by ID ONLY — never by title text.
Status: v1.9 push was DECLINED by anh at the approval card (he saw the
build going to the dabanh repo and stopped it to give this directive
first). Nothing pushed, nothing built. Awaiting his call: fold the
substitution rung into this loop before the one build, or build v1.9
as-is and do the rung next.

## OWNER DIRECTIVE — MEASURED LATENCY TUNING (2026-11-15 ~00:52 CST)
Anh: dispatch a team to audit the LATENCY of every source so switching
happens in time — never wait until the failure shows on screen. All
tuning from REAL NUMBERS: the exact wait budget for "one movie gets a
playable source". A measurement team is dispatched (VM-side, following
the apps' exact request paths) to produce per-source numbers: detail
API latency, playlist TTFB, first-segment TTFB, est. time-to-playable,
failure-declare latency per failure class, and chain-step timings
(findAlternatives, Torrentio fetch, TMDB external_ids). Output =
recommended watchdog/timeout constants per platform (tvOS 20 s stall
watchdog + request budgets; Android 12 s/15 s stall limits), set at
the healthy-start envelope (~p90 + margin), per source where variance
justifies it. Tuning lands BEFORE the tvOS build and as a constants
pass on Android before the leader's on-Sony verification.

## OWNER DIRECTIVE — INTELLIGENT PREDICTION LAYER (2026-11-15 ~00:53 CST)
Anh: "Phải đưa hệ thống thông minh vào dự đoán nó để nó hoạt động
chính xác" — static tuned constants are not enough; the app must
PREDICT per-source behaviour so switching is accurate. Leader design:
an on-device per-source health model (persisted rolling stats:
time-to-playable EWMA, failure rate by class, last-seen timestamps,
with time decay). It drives three things: (1) ladder ORDER by
predicted time-to-playable; (2) predicted-dead sources get a short
fuse instead of the full wait; (3) the stall watchdog becomes
per-source (its own predicted envelope + margin). Cold start priors =
the latency team's measured numbers; the model then adapts to the
owner's real network (tunnel/ISP), which no VM measurement can see.
This is statistics learned on-device, not guesswork. Sequencing:
measurement team (running, asked to also report variance structure)
→ predictor design → implement on both platforms through the same
gates → tvOS build stays ONE build, after the predictor passes;
Android predictor pass lands before the leader's final on-Sony
verification build.

## LATENCY AUDIT — DELIVERED 2026-11-15 01:07 CST (LATENCY-AUDIT.md, 126 timed requests)
- Answering failures declare in 0.8–2.2 s everywhere. The only slow failure is the SILENT HANG: v1/v6.streamvsmov.com black-hole VS embed fetches (TLS ok, 0 bytes ≥45 s, 3/3 over ~50 min); v8/v9 answer ~1.1–1.3 s. Client timeout constants ARE the switch speed.

- TUNING (per-stage fuses ≈3–6× slowest healthy stage): tvOS VS resolve 25→8 · TP embed 20→8 · CV 15→10 · Torrentio 35→15 · detail 25→10 · TP detail split 10+10 · findAlternatives 12 s deadline · pre-ready watchdog 20 keep · ADD 12 s mid-play freeze watchdog · proxy serve-wait 35→90 stopgap, stream-through = real fix. Android resolve reads 20→8 (CV 10) · API read 20→12 · DROP KK host C (stub-only) + 15 s total KK chain cap · STALL/POSITION/LAST_RESORT/RetryPolicy keep.
- PREDICTOR SPEC (from variance structure): stats per (provider × stage × host); streamvsmov at subdomain, blogcdn/tiktokcdn/kvp726 at domain, KK per API host; latency EWMA half-life ≈10 min; failure LATCH ≥2 consecutive timeouts → demote 15 min → single re-probe; fuse = clamp(max(3×EWMA, EWMA+4 s), floor 6 s, cap = tuned constant); priors = audit medians at low weight n≈3 (KK stream stages get NO prior — VM 404 artefact must not convict); + pooled path baseline so tunnel-wide slowness isn't misread as mass death (latch needs 3 when baseline degraded >2×). Failures cluster by host and persist; bimodality is the signature (healthy-or-absent, no middle state).

### tvOS scopes (tree ~/workspace/dabanh-ios/JoeTV/)
1. **Constants — PASS.** VS resolve cap 8 / detail 10 (VsClient.swift:25-26); KK detail 10 inside the per-host race (KkClient.swift:45); F4K detail 10 (F4kClient.swift:49); TP detail 10 + embed 8 (TpClient.swift:59,62), CV probe on its OWN budget via CacheVideo.resolve cap 10 (CacheVideo.swift:32); Torrentio streams cap 15 (TorrentClient.swift:282); JoeProxy serve-wait 90 on the playlist path only (JoeProxy.swift:241); list/search 12 unchanged in all four clients; pre-ready watchdog 20 unchanged. Gate's own stale-literal sweep of all tuned files: no surviving 25/35/20 timeout literal in any tuned class (surviving 25 s values are JoeHTTP defaults for deliberately unwired calls — TMDB fetch/imdbId, TorrentClient manifest/catalog/meta — plus subtitles 18 and RdClient 25; none is a tuned class).
2. **Mid-play freeze watchdog — PASS.** JoePlayerView.swift Coordinator: freezeTimer arms only at .readyToPlay, which also cancels the pre-ready watchdog (never overlap); paused guard (timeControlStatus == .paused covers user pause + scrub) holds the clock open and re-bases position each tick, so resume gets a fresh 12 s; end guards (DidPlayToEndTime + pos ≥ dur−1.5 s) hold open; movement >0.05 s either direction counts as alive; fire routes fireFail() → director.itemFailed() → the full ladder (refresh → hop → torrent rung → exhausted). Recorded residual (intended behaviour, not a defect): a player stalled in .waitingToPlayAtSpecifiedRate with a frozen playhead fires at 12 s — that is the failover the watchdog exists for.
3. **JoeProxy stream-through — PASS.** Offset proof verified line-by-line against stripDisguise: non-PNG decidable from the first 8 bytes (offset 0); PNG requires the TS sync triple final before any byte is served; ftyp/WebVTT verdicts deferred until the full 16 KB window is in hand so precedence cannot flip; streamDecide holds the head until off+189 bytes so detectContentType sees the same window as the whole-body path; MIME comes from detectContentType on the STRIPPED payload; non-2xx before the first byte → streamRefused → 502 (mid-stream error after bytes = connection close, documented); loopback bind (requiredInterfaceType = .loopback) intact; 512-route FIFO cap in register(); the only semaphore is confined to servePlaylist on the proxy's own queue (playlistURL never waits); playlists still whole-body rewritten.
4. **CacheVideo memo — PASS.** CacheVideo.swift:47-72: memoSet called only on found streams (successes only; Direct preferred, fallback recorded only if a stream URL exists); key "imdbId|season|episode"; TTL 600 s; flush-all above 64 entries; in-memory statics only — no stream URL is persisted anywhere.
5. **SourceHealth (tvOS) — PASS.** Half-life 600 s, weight cap 20, prior weight 3, latch window 900 s; latch on ≥2 consecutive TIMEOUTS only (success and instant-fail reset the counter and clear the latch; latch expiry does not reset the counter, so the next timeout re-latches and the next answering outcome clears); cancellation (CancellationError / URLError.cancelled) rethrown UNRECORDED; fuse = clamp(max(3×EWMA, EWMA+4 s), floor 6 s, cap) at every wired call site (VS detail+resolve, F4K detail, KK detail per-host, TP detail + Gold paging + resolveGold, CV resolve, Torrentio fetchStreams; JoeProxy records playlist/segment samples); priors are EXACTLY the 17 audit medians and there is NO KK playlist/segment prior; ordering consumers (orderServers in ladderFor, orderedAlternatives in findAlternatives) apply the stable partition on BOTH cache-hit and fresh paths, demoted last; path-baseline rule present in full (pooled baseline EWMA > 2×1.51 s AND ≥2 keys with consecutive timeouts seen within 300 s → latch threshold 3); persistence bounded (UserDefaults source_health_v1, 128-key cap, latches evicted last + LRU, saves throttled ≥2 s except latch changes); no network or UI calls in the file.
6. **findAlternatives 12 s deadline — PASS.** Providers.swift: AltSearchBox (NSLock) + unstructured task publishing progress; caller polls at 100 ms to the 12 s deadline and returns partial results; deadline-cut results are NOT cached as "not carried" (altCacheSet only on completion); search.cancel() on cut (cooperative; in-flight URLSession calls run to their own timeouts — bounded, no leak).
7. **Regression sweep — PASS WITH ONE MINOR DEFECT.** TMDBKey.swift untouched, still the empty placeholder (value = ""). project.yml:122 registers JoeTV/SourceHealth.swift; JoeTV settings stamp MARKETING_VERSION "1.9" (project.yml:151) / CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION "10" (:152) / CFBundleShortVersionString "1.9" (:158). SourceHealth/freeze references appear ONLY in the ten expected files (the seven clients + Providers + JoeProxy + JoePlayerView + SourceHealth itself) — no focus/UI file was touched beyond JoePlayerView's watchdog. Brace/paren/bracket balance 0 on all ten edited Swift files (gate re-measured with string literals and comments stripped).
   - **DEFECT (minor, pre-build):** project.yml:159 — the JoeTV Info-properties literal CFBundleVersion reads "9" while CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION (project.yml:152) is "10". ## ★ v1.9 DEVICE FAILURE — OWNER'S FIRST RUN (2026-11-15 ~11:08 CST)
- Owner installed run #14 and opened the app: Phim home sits on "Đang tải thư viện phim..." and FREEZES there (his photo: spinner + the four-provider subtitle, Phim pill focused). Same symptom class as v1.8 (Phim home stuck on spinner; v1.8 also froze the box). The desk loop (source gates + IPA dissection) did NOT catch it — every P-E item was labelled UNVERIFIED and this is the one that mattered most. Leader's failure, recorded plainly: static conformance was verified; runtime behaviour of the first-launch load path was never exercised anywhere.
- Owner's order: follow yesterday's law; identify the missing team and add SPECIALIST teams for this field.
- Response structure: NEW Team 5 (tvOS runtime forensics — trace the first-launch Phim load chain end-to-end, find the blocking mechanism with file:line evidence) + NEW Team 6 (concurrency & timeout-completeness — census of EVERY async wait reachable from launch with its real enforcement bound; adversarial focus on structured-concurrency joins that outlive their fuses and on black-hole-class servers). Then: fix round → Team 4 re-gate INCLUDING the wait-census as a gate artifact → build → dissect → deliver. No build reaches the owner until the freeze mechanism is identified and closed.
- Discriminating questions put to the owner (only he can observe): does the remote still move focus / is the spinner animating (UI alive = load hung; nothing responds = main-thread block); does the TV tab load; how long did he wait.

## OWNER'S DISCRIMINATING OBSERVATION (2026-11-15 ~11:11 CST)
- Owner's words: "Freeze toàn bộ. Remote ko tác dụng ngoài việc hold back để close app thôi" — the freeze is TOTAL: no focus movement, no remote response at all; only holding Back/Menu to force-close works.
- Diagnostic consequence: a hung async load with a live UI is RULED OUT as the primary mechanism. The main thread/main actor is blocked, deadlocked, or spinning shortly after the first frame (the spinner rendered). Both expert teams were re-briefed mid-run to prioritise synchronous blocking primitives, lock/queue deadlocks, main-actor blocking I/O, and SwiftUI update loops on the launch path, and to explicitly kill the candidates this evidence eliminates.
- Owner's verdict (2026-11-15 ~11:12 CST): "Cùng tình trạng ngày hôm qua" — v1.9's freeze is the SAME condition as v1.8's. Leader's structural admission, recorded: the v1.9 loop never root-caused v1.8's freeze mechanism; it rebuilt around the symptom (spinner ceilings, timeout constants, focus rules) and verified those statically. A mechanism that was never identified can survive a rebuild — and did. The current forensic round therefore starts from the mechanism, and no successor build may be claimed on surface conformance again.

## TEAM 6 CENSUS — COMPLETE (2026-11-15 ~11:14 CST): THE AMPLIFIER IS PROVEN, THE TRIGGER IS NOT IN THE CODE
- Full-target sweep verdict: NO mechanism in the app's own code, async or sync, reachable from launch can block the main thread indefinitely. Exactly ONE DispatchSemaphore exists (JoeProxy servePlaylist, off-main, 90 s cap, playback-only, unreachable from launch) — ELIMINATED. Zero DispatchQueue.sync / DispatchGroup.wait / NSCondition / Thread.sleep / RunLoop spins. All locks brief; all sync file reads small/local.
- PROVEN structural defect (the necessary amplifier): PhimStore is @MainActor; load(), all 24 task-group children (whose synchronous segments — parse, cache save, applyRail, mergeDedupe — inherit the main actor), the 20 s watchdog (an unstructured Task created INSIDE load(), hence main-actor-isolated; its loadTimedOut publication must win a slot on the very executor it monitors), and the view state ALL share one executor. Any stall of that executor, from any cause, yields exactly the observed joint symptom: frozen UI + spinner forever + no error surface, ever. "A ceiling checked by a tenant of the hung executor is not a ceiling."
- Systemic enforcement hole: JoeHTTP's only timeout is URLRequest.timeoutInterval (= timeoutIntervalForRequest, an idle timer reset per packet); timeoutIntervalForResource is set NOWHERE (session default 7 days) — a slow-drip peer is unbounded everywhere. PhimStore.load() has NO overall deadline (A1 UNBOUNDED as a load, though eliminated as the freeze cause). KK race join-after-cancel (A4), SourceHealth.run budget-not-a-deadline (A8), AsyncGate no acquire deadline (A3) — all NOMINAL with named holes. The one true deadline pattern in the codebase is findAlternatives' box (A10, BOUNDED).
- TRIGGER: NOT identified from source — labelled hypothesis only: the 24-job first-launch burst through URLSession.shared into the owner's Sing-box packet tunnel (platform-level stall). Needs the on-device signpost instrumentation of fix B.
- Fix shapes adopted for the fix round: A executor separation (load machinery off @MainActor; publish per rail via MainActor.run) · B independent watchdog clock + signpost timestamps · C hard 25 s overall deadline for load() via the A10 box pattern · D timeoutIntervalForResource in a dedicated JoeHTTP session config · E KK race → box pattern · F deadline race inside SourceHealth.run · G AsyncGate acquire deadline · H TVStore/TorHomeStore bounds.
- Team 5 (runtime forensics) still running; leader cross-check when it lands.

## OWNER'S TUNNEL EXPERIMENT + TEAM 7 DIFFERENTIAL — MECHANISM CONVICTED (2026-11-15 ~11:25 CST)
- Owner ran discriminator B anyway, richer than asked: TUNNEL ON → total freeze at the spinner. TUNNEL OFF → the Phim home LOADED AND RENDERED (his photo: merged KK rail with posters + "Phim chiếu rạp" rail, black background, v1.9 footer) → then CRASHED by itself. Reopen → movies still there (cache) but TOTAL FREEZE again. Next launch after force-close → fades to a black screen and CRASHES back to tvOS Home by itself.
- Diagnostic weight: with the tunnel off, data flows and renders — the network path AND the load pipeline are exonerated for good. The freeze/crash occurs WITH content on screen, i.e. in the shell/focus layer, and varies freeze↔crash between launches — consistent with a launch-time structural churn storm (hang when it saturates, system kill/crash when it doesn't survive the settle).
- TEAM 7 DIFFERENTIAL (independent, landed the same hour): the convicted mechanism is D4 — JoeMoveTrap/JoeExitTrap are view modifiers whose BODY switches structure (content vs content.onMoveCommand) based on `enabled`, and every `enabled` input is a @FocusState value the focus engine itself writes. Launch path instances: the shell topBar trap (subtree = the pills) + THREE traps on the Phim home ScrollView (subtree = the whole ScrollView). Feedback path is mechanical: initial focus lands → @FocusState flips → trap installs/uninstalls → subtree identity changes → teardown+rebuild → focus re-resolves → can re-toggle. Đá Banh TV has ZERO onMoveCommand and zero conditional modifiers. Ranked: D4 #1 (CAN), D6+D5 focus-rendering/hub cluster #2 (CAN), D8 launch fan-out #3 (contributor). Eliminated: proxy warm-start, store-init work, SourceHealth (refuted as a launch delta outright), TVStore, locks.
- Honesty label carried: the feedback loop's existence is proven from source; its non-convergence on tvOS 26/27 is inference — but it is now the only candidate that fits ALL device observations (both versions, tunnel on/off, freeze and crash variants, Đá Banh TV healthy).
- FIX ROUND OPENED with Team 7's convergence as spec: (1) traps always installed, state checked inside the handler — structural switching abolished, boundary routing preserved; (2) pills back to real Buttons (middle path: Button + per-view focusEffectDisabled + app-drawn label, keeping the look on stock structure); (3) no programmatic focus writes on the launch path; (4) root-level focusEffectDisabled dropped; (5) load staging (4 newest first, rest after first paint) + sync tails off the main actor + hard overall deadline + independent watchdog clock + signposts; (6) proxy warm-start removed from init. New Team 4 gate item: NO @FocusState-driven conditional view modifier anywhere in the target.

- Implemented per the Team 7 spec in 11 files: JoeTVApp, JoePhimHome, JoeTorrent, JoeSearch, JoeSettings, JoeTVSection, JoeStores, KkClient, SourceHealth, JoeHTTP, TorrentClient. NOT pushed, NO build dispatched — CI is the first compile.

Root .focusEffectDisabled(true) deleted. JoeProxy.shared.start() removed from init (lazy start at first play tap).
- LOAD (PhimStore): staged (4 newest jobs, then the remaining 20 after phase 1 drains — flagged interpretation of "after the first publish"); fetch+cache-save+parse run in a DETACHED worker (nonisolated runPhase), only @Published application on the main actor via a findAlternatives-style poll loop over a lock-guarded box; hard 25 s overall deadline (partial rails kept, loading always clears); AsyncGate.acquire(deadline:) fails parked waiters at the deadline; watchdog on an INDEPENDENT detached clock + LoadWatchdogBox, signposts persisted to UserDefaults "joetv.signposts" (armed / sleep done / publication attempted / publication landed / first rail landed / phase2 started / deadline hit / load completed). KK fetchJSON → KkRaceBox (winner returns without awaiting losers). SourceHealth.run enforces its budget internally (RunRaceBox race; op param now @escaping; all 9 call sites trailing closures). JoeHTTP: dedicated cached sessions with timeoutIntervalForResource == the call's timeout; TorrentClient.request rides JoeHTTP.data(for:timeout: 25). Remaining URLSession.shared uses in the target: NONE (comments only); TpClient's NoRedirect session and JoeProxy's own sessions are separate by design.
- FLAGGED follow-ups (not changed, spec-sized diff): TorHomeStore still has the same main-actor watchdog shape as old PhimStore (its load is sequential and view-gated off the launch path; Team 6 H item) — same treatment recommended next round. mergeDedupe still runs on the main actor inside applyRail (bounded, ≤~200 titles; spec 5b named save+parseList as the tails to move). 5. **CONCURRENCY — PASS (one warning-class note).** (a) AsyncGate (JoeStores.swift, private actor): exactly-once resumption HOLDS — every resume path (release / expireWaiter / cancelWaiter) removes the waiter from the actor-serialised array BEFORE resuming; pre-park cancellation resumes without queueing; the deadline warden no-ops if the waiter already left. ## TEAM 9 — DEFECT FORENSICS + FIX DESIGN FOR 1.9.2 (2026-11-15)

Scope: items 1–9 from the owner's 1.9.1 device verdict. Diagnosis + fix design ONLY — no code was edited. Every mechanism below is tied to file:line evidence read in this session; anything not provable from the tree is labelled UNVERIFIED. The profile-side split-routing fix for item 6 is out of scope (already flagged to the manager chat).

### Item 1 — White focus blobs (slate buttons, detail buttons, cards) + clipped error line

**Mechanism (one root cause for all three sub-items).** Every affected control is built the same way: a SwiftUI `Button` with `.buttonStyle(.plain)` + `.focusEffectDisabled(true)` + an app-drawn treatment. On the owner's tvOS the system white focus halo renders on these `.plain` Buttons anyway, and because the halo is drawn OUTSIDE the button's own frame it bleeds onto neighbours. This is not a new surprise: the project's own spec recorded it in v1.8 — DESIGN-SPEC-V19.md §5 F1 (lines 109–116): "coverage was complete in v1.8 and the blob still appeared; inheritance is therefore not a mechanism we rely on." The spec's F2 answer was to stop using Buttons for pills. The 1.9.1 convergence round instead rebuilt the pills as real Buttons with `.buttonStyle(.card)` (JoeTVApp.swift:260–268, PillLabel reading `@Environment(\.isFocused)`) — and the owner's photo 4 shows the focused pill rendering CLEAN (violet fill, no white blob), while every `.plain` control in photos 1/2/4 shows the blob. The sibling app settles it: Đá Banh TV (owner-verified "rất tốt" on the same box) uses `.buttonStyle(.card)` for EVERY focusable card/button (DaBanhTV/TVContentView.swift:229, 247, 270, 386, 464, 524, 595, 618) and `.plain` nowhere. The platform-level reason `.focusEffectDisabled(true)` fails to suppress the halo on `.plain` Buttons on this tvOS is labelled INFERENCE — not provable from source; the construction-level correlation (`.card` clean / `.plain` blob, across two apps and the owner's photos of this build) is the evidence the fix stands on.

- **(a) Error slate — clipped line.** JoePlayerView.swift, the exhausted-slate VStack (immediately above `slateButton` at :312): the text column carries `.frame(maxWidth:.infinity, maxHeight:.infinity, alignment:.bottomLeading)` and THEN `.padding(.leading, 48)` / `.padding(.bottom, 36)` applied OUTSIDE the flexible frame, with no trailing inset and no width cap — so the second line ("Đã thử lấy link mới và đổi nguồn nhưng vẫn không phát được.") lays out as one long line running to/past the right edge instead of wrapping. The slate's buttons are `slateButton` (JoePlayerView.swift:312+, `.plain` + `focusEffectDisabled` + JoeFocusRing) — hence the "Thử lại" blob overlapping "Quay lại".
- **(b) Detail — "Xem ngay" blob.** `playButton` (JoeMovieDetail.swift:365) and `favButton` (:389) use the same `.plain` construction. The header VStack spacing is 16, so the halo extending above the button row lands on the genre line (photo 2).
- **(c) Cards.** `PosterCard` (JoeTheme.swift:76–145) already draws the correct spec F3 treatment itself (4 pt focusViolet stroke + 1.05 scale + title colour, driven by a `focused:` Bool), but every wrapping Button is `.plain` + `focusEffectDisabled` (verified instances JoePhimHome.swift:484–486, 517–519, 573–575; the same `.plain` wrapper pattern repeats across JoeTorrent.swift — :178, 198, 230, 271, 412, 437, 546, 576 — and JoeSearch.swift:54, 73, 114, 150), so the system halo draws ON TOP of the app's own ring (photo 4's heavy white halo).

**Fix design.** Change the rendering path, not another modifier permutation (spec F9: "No third attempt with modifiers alone"):
1. Slate buttons, detail play/favourite buttons, and all PosterCard-wrapping buttons: `.buttonStyle(.plain)` → `.buttonStyle(.card)`, keeping per-view `.focusEffectDisabled(true)` and the existing app-drawn treatments (JoeFocusRing / PosterCard's F3 ring / PillLabel pattern). This mirrors the only two constructions device-proven clean: this build's pills and Đá Banh TV's cards.
2. Error slate text: cap the message column (maxWidth ≈ 1150) and move the padding INSIDE the flexible frame, so both lines wrap inside a bounded column with a trailing margin.
3. Defence in depth on the detail: raise the header gap between the genre line and the button row from 16 to 24 (a sanctioned spacing token), so even a residual halo cannot touch the text above.
- F9 tension, flagged for the leader: F9's literal text prescribes converting an artifact-showing Button class to the F2 focusable-view pattern. Route above keeps real Buttons deliberately — the slate buttons live inside the player cover, where the convergence round proved Button-ness is load-bearing for the exit rescue, and `.card` Buttons are the device-proven-clean construction in this very build (pills). If the gate judges F9 must be followed literally for cards only, cards are the one class where F2 conversion is mechanically safe (they carry no presentation semantics); buttons stay `.card` regardless.
- Risk: `.card`'s own focus animation could double with PosterCard's scaleEffect — Đá Banh TV pairs `.card` with an app-drawn `focused:` treatment on device without that defect; the gate should diff the constructions exactly. Black theme preserved (no fill changes; F3 treatments unchanged).

### Item 2 — Tunnel-off: nothing plays, and the wait is minutes long

**The ladder** (PlayDirector, JoePlayerView.swift:139–177): attempt → same-provider refresh once → cross-provider hop once → torrent rung once → exhausted slate. Per-candidate cost is bounded by the 20 s pre-ready watchdog (JoePlayerView.swift:444): a black-holing CDN burns the full 20 s per rung. Verified caps: KK detail (refresh) 10 s (KkClient.swift:41); `findAlternatives` overall deadline 12 s (Providers.swift, the box pattern ~:545–576); stream fetch race cap 20 s (JoePlayerView.swift:416–434); torrent `fetchStreams` cap 15 s via SourceHealth.

**Worst-case wait, tunnel-off KK film** (formula: rungs × 20 s watchdog + 10 s refresh detail + 12 s alternatives + hop detail):
- 1-server film: 20 (attempt) + 10 (refresh) + 20 (refreshed ladder) + 12 (alternatives) + ~10 (hop detail) + 20 (hop ladder) ≈ **92 s**.
- 3-server film (Vietsub/Thuyết minh/Lồng tiếng all in the ladder): 60 + 10 + 60 + 12 + 10 + 20 ≈ **172 s ≈ 3 minutes** before the exhausted slate. This matches the owner's "load rất lâu" and his slate photo.

**Why the walk can still end in TOTAL failure when VS/F4K/TP/CV carry the film** (all code-traced):
1. The hop tries ONLY `alts.first` — one alternative provider, never the second or third (hopIdentity closure in JoeMovieDetail.swift; PlayDirector hops once).
2. Episode matching breaks cross-provider: KK's "Full" carries episodeNumber −1 while VS canonical names are "Tập 1" (episodeNumber 1), so `Providers.ladderFor` misses by name AND by number → empty hop ladder.
3. `sameFilm` (Providers.swift) rejects a candidate when both years are non-empty and unequal — cross-provider year variants (part/year mismatches) kill the hop before it starts.
4. The torrent rung fail-fasts to `[]` when `TorrentSettings.effectiveBase == nil` (first line of `torrentFallbackCandidates`, JoeMovieDetail.swift) — zero time cost, but in the owner's test the rung did not exist at all because torrent was never configured on the ATV (his Settings photo: fields empty), which traces back to item 7 (no usable way to enter the tokens).
Whether (2)/(3) fired for HIS specific test films is UNVERIFIED — no device-side trace of those titles exists; the three are proven walk-enders in code, any one suffices.

**Home load, tunnel-off.** PhimStore.load (JoeStores.swift): 24 jobs; phase 1 = `jobs.prefix(4)`; phase 2 (20 jobs, 17 of them KK) starts ONLY after phase 1's task group fully drains (JoeStores.swift:188–213 — `runPhase(phase1)` is awaited before phase 2 begins). KK list calls cap at 12 s each (KkClient.swift:40 `listTimeout`). Tunnel-off: phase 1 ≈ 12 s (the KK job burns its full cap while the other three land and publish), then phase 2's 17 KK jobs × 12 s through AsyncGate(4) would need ~51 s, but the load's hard 25 s deadline cuts the consumer loop — late KK rails simply never land this load, and EVERY reload re-burns the same timeouts, because list calls have no health memory: SourceHealth's Stage enum is only {detail, resolve, playlist, segment} (SourceHealth.swift:64–65) — there is no list stage, so lists never consult or record the latch.

**Fix design (bounded, with numbers).**
1. PhimStore: phase 2 starts on the FIRST phase-1 publish OR after a 3 s fuse, whichever comes first (today: gated on full phase-1 drain). Non-KK catalogue complete by ~6–8 s tunnel-off instead of being cut at the 25 s deadline.
2. Add a `list` stage to SourceHealth and route the four clients' list fetches through `SourceHealth.run(stage: .list, cap: 12)`: the existing fuse (floor ~6 s) tightens repeat burns and the existing latch (2 consecutive timeouts → demote) stops a dead provider's 17 list jobs from re-burning full caps on every load.
3. Hop: iterate alternatives in order (up to 3) instead of only `alts.first`; ladderFor gains an episode-match fallback — when byName/byNum both miss and the target detail has exactly ONE playable episode ("Full"-class), use it; `sameFilm` year gate: treat a ±1 year difference as a match when title matches exactly (labelled a judgement call for the leader — the alternative is leaving year-variant hops dead).
4. No change to the torrent rung's fail-fast (0 s is correct); its absence in the owner's test is item 7's fix.

### Item 3 — Favourites: the second favourite evicts the first

**What is ELIMINATED by reading.** (i) Key collision: keys are `movieFavKey(provider:slug:)` (WatchStore.swift:101–110) with distinct provider codes KK="kk", F4K="f4k", VS="vsmov", TP="tp" (Providers.swift:19–22); the detail builds the entry with `e.key = favKey` (call site JoeMovieDetail.swift:400; torrent call site JoeTorrent.swift:768). The owner's two films have different slugs → different keys. (ii) Single-slot/dedupe/cap: the Swift `toggleFavorite` (WatchStore.swift:150–162) has no cap and no dedupe beyond same-key removal — contrast the Android original, which trims at MAX_FAVORITES (WatchStore.java:183–184); the port has no such trim. (iii) The toggle itself: device video shows the button flipping to "Đã thích", and the reopened first film's detail showing "♡ Yêu thích" again (photo 2) plus the rail holding one card (photo 4) — two independent device observations (`isFavorite` reads the same store, WatchStore.swift:144–146) prove the ENTRY left the store; the loss is in persistence, not rendering.

**The mechanism the code DOES contain.** Every mutation re-loads the entire Doc from disk, mutates it, and rewrites the entire file: `toggleFavorite` (WatchStore.swift:150–162), `saveProgress` (:122–131), `removeProgress` (:134–137) all begin `var doc = load()`. And `load()` (:185–193) swallows EVERY failure — missing file, unreadable data, ANY decode error — into an empty `Doc()`. `store()` then rewrites the whole file from that possibly-empty base, while its `defer { reload(from: doc) }` republishes the PASSED doc, so the in-session UI always looks correct and masks the loss until the next cold load. The owner's sequence contained a cold load between the two favourites: item 4's Menu bug exited the app from the first film's detail (his video's dark frames are that exit), so favourite #2's `load()` started from whatever was actually on disk — and film #1 was not in it. Net device effect of ANY round-trip failure in this design: the store can only ever hold what the last mutation wrote; the rail shows exactly one favourite.

**What is NOT pinned (labelled UNVERIFIED).** Why the on-disk doc failed to round-trip on his box. Ranked candidates: (1) stale-format file — the ATV's watch-state file predates the current `Entry` shape (fields were added across builds, e.g. the torrent CW fields `metaBase`/`metaType`), and Swift's synthesized `Decodable` does NOT honour the declared default values: one missing key fails the WHOLE Doc decode → empty base, every time, until a successful store overwrites the file. (2) A silent write failure in `store()`'s tmp+replace path (:196–210) — same masking, same net effect. The desk could not discriminate: the local tree holds only the current source, and the GitHub connector cannot resolve the older commits' file versions (short SHAs rejected as refs). The Android original is immune to candidate (1) by construction — its reader is tolerant per-field (`optString` with defaults, WatchStore.java:212–243); the Swift port's strict synthesized decoding is the regression surface.

**Fix design (kills the class under every candidate).**
1. Mutations stop re-loading from disk: the store's published arrays (already loaded at init) become the single in-memory truth; toggle/save/remove mutate in memory and persist the result. The empty-base eviction path ceases to exist.
2. Tolerant decoding: `Entry` gets a custom `Decodable` reading every field via `decodeIfPresent` + the declared defaults (mirroring the Android reader) — a file from any older/newer build can never again fail wholesale.
3. Failures become visible: a decode failure on an existing file, or a write failure, increments the Settings signpost diagnostics and preserves the unreadable file as `watch-state.unreadable.json` instead of silently returning empty — a recurrence is diagnosable from the owner's Settings screen.
**CW audit (same store, asked for):** `saveProgress`/`removeProgress` share the identical load-mutate-store shape and therefore the identical exposure; no separate CW defect is proven on device, and fix (1)+(2) covers both halves. Only two `toggleFavorite` call sites exist in the repo (grep-verified: JoeMovieDetail.swift:400, JoeTorrent.swift:768).

### Item 4 — Menu/Back semantics (REQUIRES A G-EXIT RE-GATE)

**How the detail is actually presented.** Not a cover, not a NavigationStack: the movie detail is a layer in the root ZStack (JoeTVApp.swift:157–172; torrent layer :174–183), rendered above the shell, with the shell content beneath `.disabled(nav.movie != nil || nav.tor != nil)` (:155). Its `.onExitCommand { nav.movie = nil }` sits on the WRAPPER ZStack at JoeTVApp.swift:168 (torrent: :177) — OUTSIDE JoeMovieDetail itself. JoeMovieDetail declares `.defaultFocus($focusedPlay, true)` (JoeMovieDetail.swift:119) and the owner's photo 2 shows focus living inside the detail — yet Menu exits the app. The desk gate's tree-ancestry argument ("the handler is an ancestor of the focused view, therefore it intercepts") is thereby falsified on device for the SECOND time: v1.1's NavigationStack design failed identically (documented at JoeTVApp.swift:56–60). The one placement that device-proves WORKING is the player's: `.onExitCommand { dismiss() }` at JoePlayerView.swift:309, attached inside the presented view's OWN tree, presented via fullScreenCover (JoeMovieDetail.swift:152–154). JoeSearchView's handler likewise sits on its own root (JoeSearch.swift:168). Settings needs no forensics: NO handler exists for sections anywhere — by the current G-EXIT design the root carries none — so Menu there exits by design. The precise platform reason the wrapper placement misses (responder-chain delivery of the exit command in tvOS SwiftUI) is labelled INFERENCE; the fix design deliberately stops depending on cross-subtree ancestry.

**Fix design — the owner's semantics, mirroring Android** (Back walks inside the app; exit only from the Phim root):
1. Move each layer handler INTO the view that owns the focus: `.onExitCommand { nav.movie = nil }` on JoeMovieDetail's own root; `.onExitCommand { nav.tor = nil }` on JoeTorrentDetail's own root. DELETE the wrapper handlers at JoeTVApp.swift:168 and :177.
2. Sections: section state must become settable from section views — move `section` into JoeNavigator as `@Published` (the Section enum moves with it; JoeRootView observes it). Attach an UNCONDITIONAL `.onExitCommand { nav.section = .phim }` to each NON-Phim section view's own root: JoeTVSection, JoeTorrentHome, JoeSettingsView. JoePhimHome carries NO handler; the shell root carries NO handler; JoeTVApp's root carries NO handler.
3. Player and search handlers stay exactly where they are (own roots — device-proven).
**Why the Phim-root press provably falls through:** the post-fix handler inventory is exhaustive and enumerable — player cover (own root), search (own root), movie detail (own root), torrent detail (own root), TV/Torrent/Settings section roots. At the Phim root none of these views is in the focused view's ancestry: the layers are closed, and the Phim section view itself carries no handler by construction. No app consumer exists → the press reaches the system default → exit. The gate can verify this by grepping the inventory, not by trusting an ancestry argument.
**Exactly-one-layer-per-press is preserved:** the detail/search layers are root-ZStack SIBLINGS of the section container, never descendants of section views, so a section handler is never an ancestor of layer focus; when a layer is open the section beneath is `.disabled` and holds no focus. The player is a separate presentation above the detail. Each press is consumed by exactly the innermost open layer's own handler.
**D4 safety:** every handler is unconditional and always installed; the closures check navigation state, focus state drives NO structure. This item REDEFINES G-EXIT by design (the old property "no .onExitCommand except the player's" is replaced by the inventory above) and must be re-gated as such — flagged in the fix list.

### Item 5 — Settings tunnel status (display only)

**Current state.** JoeSettings.swift has no route status anywhere (blocks present: SourceProbe diagnostics :168–223; signpost block :226–250). SourceProbe.run() (SourceProbe.swift:21–35) already probes all four providers through the real client calls (KK = KkClient.newest).
**Fix design.** A dedicated status row at the top of Settings:
- Probe: single-target KK reachability with a SHORT fuse (~6 s — note KkClient's list cap is 12 s, KkClient.swift:40, so the probe needs a timeout parameter or a race wrapper in the SourceProbe family; do not reuse the 12 s list call as-is).
- Runs when Settings appears, plus a manual "Kiểm tra lại" button (a spec F3 primary-button class). Display only — no switch, no control, matching the standing tvOS design rule.
- Copy, honestly labelled as inference: green — "Đường KKPhim: đang thông (~X ms) — đường Việt Nam đang tới được, hầm có vẻ đang bật"; red — "Đường KKPhim: không thông — kiểm tra profile hầm trên Apple TV"; while probing — "Đang kiểm tra đường KKPhim…". The label states it is inferred from KK answering, not read from the profile's actual state.

### Item 6 — Torrent section on a dead route (app side only)

**How long it spins, and what shows.** TorHomeStore.load (JoeStores.swift:618–689): manifest fetch, then a SEQUENTIAL catalogue fetch loop, then 10 genre fetches in a task group. Every discovery-class call rides JoeHTTP's DEFAULT 25 s timeout — TorrentClient.swift:176 (manifest), :213 (catalogue), :227 (genre catalogue), :241 (meta) all call `JoeHTTP.getJSON` with NO timeout argument (JoeHTTP.swift: sessions are per-timeout with `timeoutIntervalForResource == timeout`; only the subtitles call, :371 at 18 s, and the stream fetch, :290, pass explicit budgets). Unconfigured = 2 Cinemeta catalogues, so worst case ≈ 25 (manifest) + 2×25 (sequential catalogues) + 25 (genre group) ≈ **100 s** with `loading` still true. The 20 s watchdog (JoeStores.swift:642–650) sets `loadTimedOut`, and the view then swaps the spinner for the error surface (JoeTorrent.swift:88–93, :417–421) — BUT the surface's Retry calls `store.reload()`, which NO-OPS while a load is in flight (JoeStores.swift:606–609): on a dead route the owner gets the error text at ~20 s and a DEAD Retry button until the ~100 s grind drains. The current error copy already gestures at the Sing-box profile; it does not name the torrent route, and nothing bounds the wait.
**Fix design.**
1. TorrentClient discovery-class calls (manifest/catalogue/genre/meta/search) get an explicit `timeout: 8` (JoeHTTP.getJSON already accepts `timeout:`).
2. TorHomeStore's catalogue loop becomes a task group (concurrent, publish per landing — mirroring its own genre path and PhimStore's box pattern).
3. TorHomeStore gains PhimStore's hard-deadline shape at ~15 s: at the deadline `loading` clears and the error surface shows even if fetches are still in flight (late results still apply when they land). Retry is live from that moment.
4. Error copy names the likely cause: "Chưa tải được mục Torrent — đường tới Torrentio/Cinemeta đang không thông. Nếu profile hầm đang bật, đường này thường bị chặn; thử tắt hầm rồi bấm Thử lại."
New worst case to a WORKING error surface: ~15 s (deadline); typical dead-route: ~8 s (first failed round). The routing fix itself (split routing in the profile) is out of scope by the tasking.

### Item 7 — Paste path for the Torrentio / RD / TorBox fields

**Current state.** JoeSettings.field() (JoeSettings.swift:125–152) builds plain TextField/SecureField controls, focusable, `.onSubmit { save() }`, values auto-saving on change (:113–118). Nothing in the fields blocks the system's own text-input UI — the gap is purely that there is no usable path for a long token with a remote: the owner has no way to get the code in (his message was cut off mid-sentence saying exactly that).
**Fix design.** Per credential field (Torrentio URL, RD token, TorBox token): a "Dán" button beside the field that
1. reads `UIPasteboard.general.string`, trims whitespace/newlines, assigns it to the field's @State and saves (the existing auto-save path);
2. on an empty pasteboard, shows a hint under the field — "Chưa có gì trong bộ nhớ tạm — copy mã trên iPhone trước (cùng Apple ID, để gần Apple TV)" — and focuses the field as the fallback, where the system input UI offers the iPhone Remote keyboard (which itself carries paste).
Labelled UNVERIFIED (platform knowledge, not provable from this codebase): UIPasteboard is available on tvOS and Universal Clipboard reaches an Apple TV signed into the same Apple ID; iOS-style paste-access prompts are not a tvOS behaviour at the time of writing. The design is built for exactly the owner's flow — copy on iPhone → press "Dán" on the ATV — and degrades to the Remote-keyboard path if the pasteboard read ever comes back empty. This fix is also the unlock for item 2's dead torrent rung and part of item 6's testability: until tokens can be entered, neither can be exercised on device.

### Item 8 — Search unreachable

**Mechanism.** Entry: JoePhimHome's header "Tìm phim" Button (JoePhimHome.swift:199–221) sets `nav.searchOpen = true`; the body swaps grid → JoeSearchView through a Group if/else (JoePhimHome.swift:36–46), destroying the focused button's subtree. JoeSearchView declares NO `.defaultFocus` and no initial-focus declaration of any kind — the convergence round removed the focus-hub consumer and left a comment assuming "DOWN from the pills lands on the query field geometrically" (JoeSearch.swift:175–177), which only helps if focus lands on the pills; after the swap, focus has no declared landing inside the search layer at all. Contrast JoeMovieDetail, which declares `.defaultFocus($focusedPlay, true)` (JoeMovieDetail.swift:119) and lands reliably. So the search layer opens with the focus engine's fallback behaviour, which on device does not put the owner in the query field — and his exit handler (JoeSearch.swift:168, on the view's own root) is equally unreachable if focus never enters the layer. Device verdict matches exactly: "Không bấm được qua phần tìm kiếm."
**Fix design.** Add `.defaultFocus($topFocus, .field)` to JoeSearchView's body — a static, appearance-time declaration, identical in kind to the detail's proven one. D4-safe: it installs nothing conditionally and focus state drives no structure. Same class, same fix in the torrent section's search row (JoeTorrent.swift, comment at :158–161 marks the same removed hub consumer): give the torrent search field the same static defaultFocus treatment in its own FocusState.

### Item 9 — Torrent detail: "Yêu thích" cannot be pressed while the source is unconfigured

**Mechanism — NOT a configuration gate.** JoeTorrentDetail.favButton (JoeTorrent.swift, toggle call at :768) is NOT `.disabled`, and its action calls `watch.toggleFavorite(e)` UNCONDITIONALLY — there is no `isConfigured` check anywhere in the toggle path; `favKey = WatchStore.torFavKey(metaId:type:)` and `isFav` reads the same key. The defect is focus reachability, the same landing class as item 8: the favButton carries NO `.focused(...)` binding and JoeTorrentDetail declares NO `.defaultFocus` anywhere. For an UNCONFIGURED movie title, `loadStreams` early-returns (JoeTorrent.swift:975–980), the stream section renders only the "Chưa cấu hình nguồn" notice (:941–946), and there is no episode section — so the favourite button is the ONLY focusable element in the layer, unbound and un-defaulted, and focus never lands on it. When the source IS configured, stream rows (bound to `focusedRow`) exist, focus lands there, and geometric UP can reach the hero — which is why the defect only shows in the unconfigured state the owner photographed.
**Same-screen audit.** Episode chips and both retry buttons DO carry `.focused($focusedRow, equals:)` bindings (episodeList :848–877; retryButton :816–833); the stream rows are bound. The favButton is the only unbound focusable on the screen. JoeMovieDetail comparison: its favButton (JoeMovieDetail.swift:389) is likewise unbound, but its sibling play button carries that view's `.defaultFocus` (:119), so focus enters the header row and RIGHT reaches the favourite — the movie detail works by that accident of geometry; the torrent detail has no such carrier in the unconfigured state.
**Fix design.** Bind the torrent favButton into the detail's existing FocusState — `.focused($focusedRow, equals: "fav")` — and declare `.defaultFocus($focusedRow, "fav")` on JoeTorrentDetail's body. Favouriting then works regardless of configuration (it already did logically; now it is reachable). Side effect, stated plainly: on CONFIGURED titles the initial landing becomes the favourite button (topmost action; one DOWN reaches the stream rows), replacing today's implicit landing. D4-safe: a static binding + a static appearance-time default; nothing conditional is installed or removed.

- JoeTVSection.swift:229 — group chip (`chip(_:)`): `.focused($focusedChip, equals: group)`; treatment = accentViolet capsule fill + 4pt joeFocusViolet strokeBorder + scale 1.05.
- JoeTVSection.swift:250 — action chip (`actionChip`): `.focused($focusedChip, equals: id)`; treatment = accentViolet fill when focused + 4pt stroke + scale 1.05.
