## TEAM UI-1 — DESIGN DISSECTION, IMAGE DIAGNOSIS, DESIGN SYSTEM + MOCKUP (2026-11-15)

Scope: dissection + diagnosis + spec + mockup ONLY. No app code edited. Mockup files: `JoeTV/ui-redesign/` (style.css, home.html, search.html, detail.html, slate.html, dialog.html, posters/ — real KK posters fetched this session). **PNG renders could NOT be produced: the only Chromium on this VM is the managed live-browser build (/opt/meta-chromium/chrome), which refuses standalone startup ("Hatch WebGL domain policy is required for browser startup"); no other headless renderer (chromium-browser, google-chrome, wkhtmltoimage, weasyprint, firefox) exists on the VM. The HTML files are the deliverable renders — open them at 1920×1080.**

### PART A — Why Đá Banh renders clean and Joe TV slabs: the construction difference

**What `.buttonStyle(.card)` actually is (the platform contract).** On tvOS the card button style is not a neutral wrapper: the SYSTEM draws its own background platter behind the label (a light material rounded rect with the style's own corner radius and shadow) and owns the focus transform (platter brightens + the whole button scales on focus). A `.card` label is designed to be content painted over — or fully covering — that platter. Every focusable in BOTH apps uses `.card` (Đá Banh: 8 sites, TVContentView.swift + TVMatchViews.swift; Joe TV: all focusables since 1.9.2). The style is therefore NOT the differentiator. What each app does AROUND the style is:

**Đá Banh's construction (device-proven clean — the whole recipe):**
1. `.buttonStyle(.card)` and NOTHING else on the button: **zero `.focusEffectDisabled`, zero `.scaleEffect` in the entire DaBanhTV target** (grep-verified this session: 0 and 0). The style's own focus presentation is left alone.
2. Every label paints its ENTIRE frame with ONE shape: `tvCardBackground(focused:)` (TVMatchViews.swift:170-179) — a single `RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius: 22)` filled with a white .13→.05 gradient, applied via `.background(...)` to a FIXED frame (cards 470×252, featured 640×330), with the focus shown ONLY by value changes inside that always-installed background: stroke cyan 4pt focused / white .16 1.5pt unfocused. Title colour is a value too (white → cyan #00E5FF when focused, TVMatchViews.swift:340). Text always sits on the label's own painted face, never on the raw platter.
3. Pills: label paints a full `Capsule().fill(selected ? tealFill : white.opacity(0.10))`, text always `.white` (TVContentView.swift tabPill). No scale, no ring overlays, no focus-effect suppression.
4. No text fields exist anywhere in Đá Banh TV — the search-field defect class cannot occur there.

**Joe TV's construction — three fights against the same style, one per defect cluster:**
1. **In-label scaleEffect (25 sites)** — PosterCard `.scaleEffect(focused ? 1.05)` (JoeTheme.swift:145), pills 1.06 (JoeTVApp.swift PillLabel), detail play/alt/episode buttons 1.05 (JoeMovieDetail.swift:398/456/513), slate buttons 1.05 (JoePlayerView.swift:334), search buttons (JoeSearch.swift), Settings + TV section + Torrent (25 total, grep-counted). The `.card` style ALSO transforms the button on focus; the label scaling 1.05 INSIDE a button the system is separately transforming makes label and platter scale by different factors around the centre → they misregister exactly at the corners and edges.
2. **Text over the raw platter** — PosterCard's face is `RoundedRectangle.fill(white.opacity(0.07))` (JoeTheme.swift:138-144): a 7% tint, effectively transparent. The poster's text block (title + meta) sits in the VStack BELOW the art with nothing opaque behind it, so on focus the system's brightened platter IS the text's background. The focused title colour is `focusViolet` #B894FF (0.72, 0.58, 1.00 — relative luminance ≈ 0.386) on a pale platter (luminance ≈ 0.6) → contrast ≈ **1.5 : 1** → "pale violet on pale grey, sunk". Đá Banh's focused title is cyan (luminance ≈ 0.632) on its own dark painted face (≈ black + 7–13% white, luminance ≈ 0.006–0.02) → contrast ≈ **12 : 1**.
3. **Stacked shapes with three radii** — PosterCard stacks the poster clip `RoundedRectangle(16)` (JoeTheme.swift:106), the card background `RoundedRectangle(16)` (:139), and the `.card` platter's own system radius, plus an inner black@0.45 rect under the AsyncImage (:88-89). Focused, the platter (different radius, light) extends past the label's 16pt face at the corners → the pale crescents the owner photographed as "grey residue at rounded corners", and the focused silhouette is the platter's shape while the unfocused silhouette is the label's 16pt rect → "selected card's rounding differs from unselected". Đá Banh has exactly ONE shape per card (22pt) in both states; only its stroke changes.

**Defect-by-defect attribution (owner's five photos):**
- **(1) White search slab + sunk text** — JoeSearch.swift:24-37: a native `TextField` (`.textFieldStyle(.plain)`, WHITE text, white@0.07 fill). A tvOS text field's FOCUSED rendering is drawn by the system control itself — a light/white rounded field — and `.focusEffectDisabled(true)` (JoeSearch.swift:37) does not suppress the control's own focused fill (proven by his photo: the slab appears despite the modifier). White text on the system's white focused field = sunk. Same construction in Settings token fields (JoeSettings.swift:146-160) → same latent defect. Đá Banh has no text fields at all.
- **(2) Pale focused-card slab + pale-violet title** — `.card` platter showing through the 7% face (JoeTheme.swift:139) + focusViolet-on-pale contrast ≈1.5:1 (above). The 1.9.2 gate checked that treatments EXIST, not what the composite renders as — the leader's recorded gate limitation.
- **(3) "2026 · Tập 2" clipped at the card's bottom edge** — the meta line is the LAST element of PosterCard's VStack (JoeTheme.swift:127-136), sitting at the very bottom of the label with only the 12pt padding below it; the in-label 1.05 scale (JoeTheme.swift:145) grows the label about its centre, pushing the bottom line ~12pt further down — past the platter's bottom edge and into the rail row's bounds (JoeTheme.swift Rail: `.padding(.vertical, 16)` on a top-aligned LazyHStack, JoeTheme.swift:168-176) — while the platter behind it does not grow by the same factor. First line to cross the edge is the meta. (The double-scale mechanism is source-proven; the exact pixel clip chain is inference from the photo description — labelled.)
- **(4) Grey residue at rounded corners** — the three-radius stack (above): platter radius ≠ 16pt label radius, misregistered further by the double scale.
- **(5) Selected ≠ unselected rounding/clipping** — unfocused silhouette = label's 16pt rect + 2pt white@0.10 stroke; focused silhouette = the system's platter shape + the 1.05-scaled label. Two different shapes by construction. In Đá Banh both states are the same 22pt shape; only stroke colour/width values change.

**The one-line verdict of Part A:** Joe TV 1.9.2 kept the v1.9 app-drawn focus system (own ring + own scale + focus effect suppressed) and bolted it onto a button style whose entire design is to own focus presentation itself; Đá Banh cooperates with the style (paints a full face, changes only values, never scales, never suppresses). Every one of the five defects is a seam between those two systems.

### PART B — Image pipeline diagnosis, with numbers

**The path today (source).** Posters render through SwiftUI `AsyncImage` — one instance inside PosterCard (JoeTheme.swift:92), reused by every rail (JoePhimHome.swift:492/529/567, JoeSearch.swift grid), plus detail/backdrop sites (JoeMovieDetail.swift ×4, JoeTVSection.swift ×2, JoeTorrent.swift ×1). There is NO app-level image loader, no in-flight dedupe, no decoded-bitmap cache, no prefetch. `JoeTVApp.init` replaces `URLCache.shared` with 64 MB memory / 512 MB disk (JoeTVApp.swift:24-26) — a cache exists ON PAPER, and the CDN cooperates (below) — but every card appearance creates a fresh AsyncImage task, a card leaving the LazyHStack viewport cancels it, and re-appearing restarts it; AsyncImage also decodes at FULL image resolution (PosterCard applies `.resizable().scaledToFill()` AFTER decode, JoeTheme.swift:95).

**Measurements (this VM, direct egress, 2026-11-15).** Source: the app's own call — GET `https://phimapi.com/v1/api/danh-sach/phim-moi-cap-nhat?page=1` (24 items); posters resolve to `https://phimimg.com` (the API's APP_DOMAIN_CDN_IMAGE, KkClient.swift:38/255). Sample of the first 12 posters, downloaded with curl:
- Bytes: 34,948 … 977,716 B; median ≈ 84 KB; mean ≈ 238 KB; 12 posters = 2.86 MB total.
- Dimensions: 7 of 12 are 500×750-class (well matched to the 240×360pt card); 2 are 1440×2160 (156 KB, 325 KB); 2 are 2000×3000 (880 KB JPEG, 978 KB WebP); 1 is 549×835. Decoded bitmap cost: 500×750 = 1.5 MB; 1440×2160 = 12.4 MB; 2000×3000 = **24 MB per image**.
- Time (second pass, per-file): totals 0.60–1.21 s with **TTFB alone 0.48–0.80 s** — a 46 KB poster took 0.87 s while the 978 KB poster took 1.21 s: the load is LATENCY-bound, not byte-bound (21× the bytes cost only ~0.3 s more). First (cold) pass ran slower still (first poster 1.50 s).
- Headers (phimimg.com, Cloudflare): `Cache-Control: public, max-age=2592000, immutable` + ETag — the origin EXPLICITLY allows 30-day caching. The cache misses on device are therefore the app's loading shape, not the CDN's policy.
- Variants: the API offers `poster_url` (portrait) + `thumb_url` (landscape, 780×438 measured, 36 KB) — the app already keeps them apart correctly (KkClient.swift:307-308). phimimg.com honours NO resize parameter (`?w=240` returned byte-identical output, verified) — downscaling can only happen at decode on-device. (Tủ Phim already ships a 300px variant and TpClient.swift:118 prefers it.)

**Attribution of the ~10-minute loads (ranked):**
1. **Latency × per-appearance restarts, no dedupe (DOMINANT).** Floor from this VM is ~0.6–1.2 s PER poster, latency-dominated. AsyncImage starts on appearance, cancels on disappearance; LazyHStack recycling means ordinary scrolling repeatedly aborts and restarts the same URLs. URLSession.shared's default per-host concurrency is 6, so a 10-poster rail needs ≥2 sequential rounds ≈ 2 s best case FROM THIS VM — and on the owner's Apple TV the same requests additionally traverse the Sing-box tunnel (the KK image CDN is reached through the tunnel profile — the profile routes KK via the tunnel; exact tunnel throughput on his box is UNVERIFIED from here; the device-side check that settles it: time the same rail with the tunnel profile off vs on). Rounds of restarts × tunnel RTT is how a screen of posters stretches toward his ~10 minutes.
2. **Full-resolution decode of oversized originals.** A third of the catalogue's posters are 1440×2160 or 2000×3000 (up to 24 MB decoded each) for a 240×360pt card. Decode time, memory pressure, and cache churn scale with pixels the screen never shows.
3. **Cache that cannot be hit in the shape loads happen.** URLCache is configured and the origin allows caching, yet the device visibly re-fetches on revisit/relaunch. The desk cannot prove which link breaks on device (AsyncImage/shared-session cache interaction vs memory-tier eviction under 24 MB bitmaps vs cancellation discarding partial loads) — labelled UNVERIFIED; the fix below removes the dependence on all three by making loading the app's own explicit pipeline.
4. **No prefetch.** Rails render their metadata instantly from the disk rail cache (JoeStores.swift PhimRailCache) but their posters start loading only when each card scrolls into view — the worst possible moment.

**The fix design (sized by the measurements):**
- **One image pipeline** (replaces AsyncImage for posters/backdrops): an app-owned loader on a dedicated `URLSession` (own configuration: `httpMaximumConnectionsPerHost = 8` for image hosts, `timeoutIntervalForResource` bounded), all work off the main actor; views observe results, never fetch.
- **In-flight dedupe + no cancel-on-disappear:** one task per URL, many observers; a card leaving the screen detaches its observer, the fetch finishes into the cache. Revisits are free.
- **Two cache tiers, both persistent where it matters:** (a) DATA tier = the existing URLCache configuration (64 MB mem / 512 MB disk, `joetv-images`) used EXPLICITLY by the pipeline's session with `.returnCacheDataElseLoad` — the origin's `immutable, max-age=2592000` makes entries valid 30 days; refresh rule: honour origin headers, no app-side TTL needed; eviction = URLCache's own LRU at the 512 MB cap (≈ 2,000+ posters at the measured mean). (b) DECODED tier = `NSCache<NSURL, UIImage>` capped ≈ 120 MB total cost, keyed by URL + target pixel size — kills re-decode on scroll revisits.
- **Downscale at decode:** decode with ImageIO thumbnailing to the display size (card 240×360pt → ≤480×720px @2x; backdrop ≤1920 wide) — a 2000×3000 original becomes a 1.4 MB bitmap instead of 24 MB; decode off-main, always.
- **Prefetch:** when a rail's movies land (the store already knows them), prefetch that rail's posters + the next rail's, in rail order, at lower priority than visible cards. Home's first two rails ≈ 20 posters ≈ one 3-round pass ≈ seconds, once — then disk-cached for 30 days.
- Expected effect (labelled estimate): cold first-ever home ≈ one latency pass over ~240 posters at 8-wide ≈ 30 rounds × ~1 s ≈ 30 s worst case from VM-like latency (less with prefetch ordering — visible rails first); every later launch/scroll = cache hits, posters present with the rails. The owner's acceptance test ("load lại phải hiện sẵn") is exactly the cache-hit path.

### PART C — The design system (one page) + mockup

**Colours — Đá Banh TV's exact values** (extracted from DaBanhTV/TVMatchViews.swift TVPalette + TVContentView.swift):
- Accent green (selected pills, primary buttons, badges): `tealFill` = RGB (0.05, 0.36, 0.30) = **#0D5C4D**. (Đá Banh's own primary-button use on black lifts it slightly; the spec's primary-button token is the same hue at #0E7A64 for the black foundation — one hue family, two stops, stated openly.)
- Focus colour (rings, focused titles): `cyan` = RGB (0, 0.898, 1) = **#00E5FF**.
- Meta-on-dark accent text: `heroMeta` = (0.75, 0.94, 0.90) = #BFF0E6 (rail subtitles/badges where a soft green-white is wanted).
- Foundation: Joe TV keeps PURE BLACK (the owner's v1.9 black ruling stands; Đá Banh's teal GRADIENT background is NOT adopted — it is also a distinguisher).
- Violet is retired everywhere (accent, focus, selected fills). Red stays only as the live/error accent (#FF453A family, unchanged semantics).

**One corner-radius scale:** pills/buttons = Capsule (full round); cards & dialog = **22** (Đá Banh's card radius); fields & rows = **18**; poster art inside a card = 22 − 12 inset = **10** (concentric). ONE shape per control, drawn once as the control's background; the focused state changes stroke colour/width VALUES inside that same shape — so the silhouette and clipping are bit-identical in both states (the owner's requirement, and Đá Banh's tvCardBackground pattern verbatim).

**Focus treatment per class (built ONLY from Part A's device-clean constructions):** every focusable = `Button` + `.buttonStyle(.card)`, NO `.focusEffectDisabled`, NO in-label `.scaleEffect` (both banned by this spec — they are the 1.9.2 defect mechanism); the label paints its full frame with the class's single shape; focus = cyan stroke 4pt (vs white .16 / 1.5pt at rest) + title/accent colour → cyan where the class has a title. Focus inputs remain rendering VALUES (`focused:` Bool / @Environment isFocused) inside always-installed backgrounds — no structural conditional modifiers anywhere (convergence law intact; nothing in this system pressures it — flagged explicitly: the design needs NO exception to the D4 ban).

**Card anatomy (PosterCard v2):** one `RoundedRectangle(22)` surface (white .13→.05 gradient over black, as Đá Banh) as the background of the WHOLE card; poster art inset 12, clipped at radius 10, 240×360; title INSIDE the surface under the art (25pt semibold, 1 line, white → cyan when focused); meta line inside the surface (23pt, white .62) with 14pt of surface below it — the text lives on the painted face with margin on every side, so no state can push it across an edge; progress bar and provider badge keep their places inside the art's clip. Fixed card width 264; height is content height (~486) and IDENTICAL in both states.

**Contrast (checked against BOTH backgrounds):** focused title cyan #00E5FF (L≈0.632) on the card face over black (L≈0.006–0.02) ≈ **12:1**; white text on green #0D5C4D (L≈0.083) ≈ **7.9:1**; meta white .62 on black ≈ 7:1; field text #141416 on the field's #F5F5F7 ≈ **15:1**. The retired pairing (focusViolet L≈0.386 on the pale platter L≈0.6 ≈ 1.5:1) is recorded as the anti-pattern the system exists to prevent: NO light colour is ever placed on a surface that can lighten.

**Fields (search + Settings tokens) — designed WITH the platform:** a tvOS text field's focused rendering is system-drawn light (Part A, defect 1); fighting it produced the slab. The system's fields are therefore LIGHT BY DESIGN in both states: fill #F5F5F7 (opaque), text #141416, placeholder #6E6E73, radius 18, cyan 4pt ring when focused. If the platform paints its own light focused field, it matches the design; if it respects ours, it also matches — text can never sink in either rendering. This mirrors Apple TV's own search-field grammar.

**Text-fitting rules:** titles 1 line with ellipsis INSIDE the card surface (never across its edge); meta always inside the surface with bottom margin ≥14; detail header gap 24 (kept from 1.9.2); slate text column capped at 1150 (kept); no text element may be the last thing touching a control's bounds in any state — every text block carries its own padding inside its painted shape.

**Distinguishability from Đá Banh TV (shared green, different app):** Joe TV = pure-black foundation (Đá Banh: teal gradient), JOE TV wordmark with the glowing play-triangle in the O (kept, triangle now cyan/green), top pill bar + 2:3 poster rails (Đá Banh: crest face-off hero + landscape match tiles), film catalogue content. Same accent family, unmistakably different silhouette — the owner's standing rule (never open the wrong app) is preserved by layout + branding, not by colour.

**Mockup (1920×1080, same layout geometry as the current app, real KK posters):** `ui-redesign/home.html` (Phim home — pills + two rails, second card of rail 1 FOCUSED with cyan ring + cyan title, neighbours unfocused — the owner's photo-4 comparison), `search.html` (light field focused with sample text, Tìm/Quay lại, results grid, first card focused), `detail.html` (backdrop, poster, Xem ngay focused / Yêu thích), `slate.html` (error slate, Thử lại focused), `dialog.html` (exit dialog over dimmed home — addendum below), shared `style.css`, posters in `ui-redesign/posters/`. PNG rendering was impossible on this VM (see the note at the top of this section) — the HTML files at 1920×1080 ARE the mockup deliverable.

### ADDENDUM — Exit confirmation dialog (owner order, 2026-11-15)

**Behaviour:** Menu at the Phim ROOT no longer falls through to the system. A new, always-installed `.onExitCommand` on the Phim section root (JoePhimHome's root) consumes the press and — state-checked inside the closure (no layer open above it can be true when focus is in the section, because open layers carry their own nearer handlers) — presents the confirmation. All other Back stops keep the 1.9.2 semantics (layers close one level; TV/Torrent/Settings sections return to Phim). **G-EXIT inventory grows 7 → 8; this site must be re-gated as an inventory amendment, and the "Phim root has no consumer" property is retired and replaced by "Phim root's only consumer is the exit confirmation".** Exactly-one-layer-per-press is preserved: the section handler is an ancestor only of section focus; detail/player/search/dialog presentations sit above it with their own handlers.

**Presentation mechanism:** the platform's standard dialog — SwiftUI `.alert` (tvOS renders it as the centred system modal) bound to a presentation @State on the Phim root. Presentation state is NOT focus state; installing it changes no structure conditionally on focus (D4-clean). While the alert is up, the system owns focus and Menu dismisses the alert (platform behaviour — the "Ở lại" path needs no code).

**Anatomy (design target — the system renders the real dialog's chrome; the app supplies title, message, and the two buttons):** title "Thoát Joe TV?"; message "Phim đang xem dở vẫn được lưu lại cho lần mở sau."; buttons: "Thoát" (role .destructive) and "Ở lại" (role .cancel). **Default focus on "Ở lại"** — via the cancel role, which tvOS alerts focus by default (platform behaviour; device-verify in the P-E list). The mockup render (dialog.html) draws the design-system card (radius 22, surface gradient, cyan ring on the focused "Ở lại", green primary treatment) over the dimmed Phim home as the visual target if a future round ever hand-draws it; under the standard mechanism the delivered pixels are the system's own dialog rendering, which is dark, centred, and cannot reproduce the slab defects (it is not an app-drawn .card surface). Colours/typography of the app-supplied texts follow the system tokens above.

**How "Thoát" terminates the app — survey + recommendation.** tvOS has NO public quit API (no `UIApplication.terminate`, no exit entitlement for normal apps). What sideloaded/tvOS apps actually do:
- (a) `exit(0)` (Darwin) after flushing state — the common sideload route: the process ends, tvOS returns to the Home screen. Risks, labelled: termination is immediate — no `applicationWillTerminate`/scene-phase callbacks run, so anything not yet persisted is lost; in Joe TV the exposure is bounded (WatchStore persists synchronously on every mutation since the 1.9.2 rewrite; the player flushes progress on its existing disappear path — the Thoát action must call the same flush first; the rail disk cache is last-good data, losing an in-flight write is harmless). tvOS shows no "quit unexpectedly" UI for this; diagnostics may log the exit as an abnormal termination (cosmetic for a sideloaded app; it WOULD be an App Store rejection, which is out of scope — the owner sideloads).
- (b) Private API (`UIApplication` suspend via selector) — fragile across tvOS versions, can hang on a black screen instead of exiting; NOT recommended.
- (c) `abort()` / deliberate crash — pollutes diagnostics, can trigger system crash UI behaviour; rejected.
- (d) No termination — dismiss the dialog and tell the user to press Menu again: after "Ở lại"-style dismissal the root has a consumer now, so a second Menu would just reopen the dialog — this route cannot work with the confirmation installed; rejected.
- **RECOMMENDATION: (a)** — on "Thoát": run the app's existing flush points (player progress flush if a player is somehow active — it cannot be, the dialog is only reachable at the Phim root — and a final WatchStore persist, which is already synchronous), dismiss the alert, then `exit(0)` on the next runloop tick. Risk label: abrupt-termination data exposure is bounded to the rail cache's in-flight write (harmless, last-good design); everything the owner owns (favourites, progress, settings) is already durable before the call.

### Honesty declaration
- **Claims:** Part A's construction facts (file:line + grep counts: Đá Banh scaleEffect 0 / focusEffectDisabled 0; Joe TV scaleEffect 25); Part B's measurements (12-poster sample: bytes, dimensions, per-file times, TTFB; phimimg.com cache headers; no-resize verification); Part C's colour values (read from TVPalette/TVContentView source) and contrast arithmetic; the dialog's handler-placement and G-EXIT consequences; the exit-route survey.
- **Evidence:** source reads and greps run this session (all cited inline); curl measurements against the app's own API call and CDN; the mockup HTML files with the real fetched posters.
- **Unverified, labelled:** (i) the pixel-level rendering chain of the .card platter on the owner's tvOS (the desk cannot see tvOS rendering — the leader's recorded gate limitation; Part A's attribution rests on the two apps' construction difference + his photos, and the double-scale/corner/clip chain is mechanism inference where marked); (ii) which link of the cache chain breaks on device (Part B cause 3) and the tunnel's exact share of the 10-minute loads (cause 1) — the device-side tunnel on/off timing check that settles both is named in Part B; (iii) PNG renders do not exist (renderer unavailable on this VM, stated plainly at the top); (iv) tvOS alert default-focus-on-cancel and Menu-dismisses-alert are platform behaviours to device-verify in the P-E list; (v) `exit(0)` behaviour is the documented sideload pattern, not tested on his box.

## RESTRUCTURE ORDER (2026-11-15 ~14:52 CST, via manager chat): TEAMS MERGE, THEME = LAYOUT, TECH GATE, PRESENTATION SPEC
- UI-2 (ideas) and UI-1 (UI) produce ONE joint product — UI-1 is complete (board section above); UI-2 must design within UI-1's proven construction rules and image-pipeline design.
- THEME = a complete system INCLUDING its own layout (layout may change entirely per theme), not a colour swap on one skeleton. This SUPERSEDES UI-2's original "one skeleton, ten token sets" brief. Each theme states where its layout differs from the current app.
- A TECHNICAL GATE team (UI-3, to be dispatched when the theme pack lands) reviews every theme before presentation: focus-model soundness under the convergence law, feasibility against real source data, performance budget (image cache, no heavy effects). Failures are excluded or presented with explicit technical-cost notes.
- Presentation deliverable (owner to leader): renders of the SAME screen set (Home, grid, Detail, Search, Player, Settings/exit dialog) with the SAME content for every theme; per theme — name, one-line concept, survey origin, layout deltas, 2–3 concrete strengths, honest trade-offs, tech-gate note; plus a 10-theme comparison table and the teams' recommended top 3 with reasons. Owner picks the final 3. No code before the pick.

## TEAM UI-2 — SURVEY, REDESIGN PLAN, 10 THEME SAMPLES (2026-11-15)

Scope: research + plan + HTML renders ONLY. No app code edited. Built WITHIN Team UI-1's proven construction rules and measured data (board section above): one painted shape per control, focus = stroke/colour VALUE changes inside the always-installed shape (no in-label scale, no focusEffectDisabled, no silhouette change between states), fields light by design, one radius scale per theme, text always inside its painted surface with margins, image pipeline = UI-1's app-owned loader + two-tier persistent cache + rail prefetch (every layout below assumes posters arrive from that cache; the uncached state is designed as the painted card surface with title/meta already present and a quiet ▶ glyph in the art area — shown deliberately as card 6 of rail 1 on every Home render). Renders: HTML/CSS at 1920×1080 — the VM has NO usable PNG renderer (the only Chromium is the managed live-browser build, policy-gated; UI-1 established the same), so the HTML files ARE the renders, exactly as UI-1 delivered theme 1's seed.

### PART 1 — Survey: proven patterns, per app (patterns only, no branding/artwork copied)
- **Apple TV app (tvOS):** content-first canvas, artwork carries the colour while chrome stays quiet; top tab bar (3–7 destinations) that reveals on demand; detail = backdrop-led page with poster, meta row, action row, episodes/cast below the fold; focus = lift/brighten/scale by the SYSTEM style — the app paints full faces and lets values change. Lesson adopted: restraint of chrome; backdrop-led detail; safe areas 80pt sides.
- **Netflix (tvOS, current layout):** shortcuts moved to a TOP navigation area; billboard hero on Home for one featured title; personal hub (My Netflix) pulls continue-watching/saved forward; rows of portrait posters with stable card sizes and predictable D-pad geometry. Lessons adopted: hero-first home option (theme 2), top-nav clarity, CW prominence.
- **Plex (tvOS/Android TV):** left SIDEBAR navigation for libraries/sources; home = hero + rails; detail with cast/synopsis/more-like-this; graphite surfaces + one strong accent. Lessons adopted: sidebar for a multi-source aggregator (theme 3), graphite+orange system (theme 10), split poster hero.
- **Infuse (tvOS):** library-first minimalism — poster grids, rich metadata on detail (synopsis, cast, ratings), very quiet chrome, favourites/library organisation over editorial noise. Lessons adopted: minimal tab chrome (theme 4), metadata-complete detail anatomy shared by all themes.
- **Jellyfin (tvOS/Android TV):** sections as poster grids with filter chips; "Next Up / Continue Watching" shelves lead with recency; library tiles carry real poster art. Lessons adopted: grid screen with lang filter chips (all themes), CW shelf ordering in the plan.
- **Stremio (TV):** Board = catalogue rails by source/addon; detail separates metadata from STREAM SOURCE selection (streams listed per addon) — the closest surveyed cousin to Joe TV's multi-provider reality. Lesson adopted: the detail page's explicit "Nguồn phát" server-chip row (KK/VS/F4K/TP/Torrent) in every theme — source choice is a first-class row, not buried.
- **Disney+ / Max / Prime Video (tvOS):** brand-led dark homes, big hero, collections rails; Max = deep-blue foundation; all use white-or-accent focus rings at 4pt+ with title colour change. Lessons adopted: deep-blue system (theme 8), ring+title focus grammar everywhere.
- **Platform guidance (Apple HIG tvOS / Google TV design / UX references):** body text 29pt class at 1080p; focus must be unmistakable at 3 m — scale is the system's, the app's job is stroke/brightness/colour values; strict grid geometry so D-pad moves are predictable; hero must leave at least one rail visible; NEVER pure-white text surfaces that can bloom — and Joe TV's own device history adds: never a light colour on a surface that can lighten (UI-1's 1.5:1 anti-pattern).
- **In-house baseline (Đá Banh TV, device-proven):** .card style left alone; label paints its full frame with ONE shape (r=22); focus = cyan stroke 4pt vs white .16/1.5pt + title → cyan; pills = full capsule fills (tealFill selected); zero scaleEffect, zero focusEffectDisabled in the whole target (UI-1 Part A).

### PART 2 — Source-data reality (designs are fitted to THESE numbers)
- **KK newest list, live sample this session (48 items, 2 pages of the app's own call):** EVERY item carries name, origin_name, poster_url, thumb_url, year, quality, lang, episode_current, category[], country[], time, type, tmdb/imdb ids. quality = "FHD" on 48/48 sampled. lang ∈ {Vietsub, Vietsub + Lồng Tiếng, Vietsub + Thuyết Minh, + both}. episode_current is a finished label ("Tập 2", "Tập 229"). Featured-film detail (real, fetched): origin name, runtime "45 phút/tập", episode_total 8, genres (Chính Kịch · Tâm Lý · Tình Cảm), actors list, Vietnamese synopsis (~700 chars — detail layouts budget 3 lines + ellipsis).
- **Titles (Vietnamese name):** min 8, median 20, mean 23.3, MAX 71 chars (multi-name concatenations with " / "). Card title = 1 line, ellipsis INSIDE the surface (at 25pt in a 240pt art width ≈ 17–19 chars visible; the 45-char "Hiệp Sĩ Mặt Nạ MY-TH: Huyền Thoại 12 Con Giáp" is rendered in the set as the ellipsis proof). Detail title can wrap 2 lines (71-char worst case budgeted).
- **Posters:** 2:3 portrait, 500×750 class dominant (7/12 in UI-1's sample; 5/8 in this session's fresh sample), outliers 1440×2160 and 2000×3000 (880–978 KB, 24 MB decoded). Bytes: median ≈ 84 KB, mean ≈ 238 KB (UI-1, 12 posters, curl). Thumbs (backdrop use): 780×438/439, ~31–76 KB typical (one 3840×2160 outlier at 519 KB). phimimg.com serves NO resize variants — the pipeline downscales at decode (UI-1 Part B); card art is designed at 240–264pt wide (≤528px @2x target), hero backdrop ≤1920 wide.
- **Other sources (from their clients):** VS = KK-shaped fields (poster_url/thumb_url/year/quality/lang/episode_current). F4K = TMDB poster URLs (2:3, w500 class), year, episode numbers. Tủ Phim (TP) = posterPath300 preferred (300px variant exists — the one source that ships a card-sized image), quality/lang/episodeCurrent. Torrent (Cinemeta) = poster + year + type; episode data per-season. Card anatomy is therefore source-agnostic: art 2:3 + FHD-class quality badge + "year · episode" meta + provider badge when aggregated — every field exists on every source.
- **Render content set (identical across all 10 themes):** featured/detail film = "Yêu Là Tàn Nhẫn" with its REAL fetched metadata + synopsis (truncated as the app would); 12 rail/grid films with real titles/posters/year/episode from the live list; search query "tình"; player = the real exhausted-source slate copy from 1.9.2; settings = the real 1.9.2 rows (Giao diện, Đường KKPhim status line, Nguồn Torrent, the two light token fields + "Nhập từ iPhone", Nhật ký chẩn đoán).

### PART 3 — The joint plan (one product with UI-1)
- **Shared skeleton components (all themes):** top/side navigation to the 4 sections + search; Home (hero per theme + CW shelf first when non-empty + rails); film grid with lang filter chips; Detail (poster, meta, genres, synopsis, Xem ngay/Yêu thích, Nguồn phát chips, Chọn tập chips); Search (light field + recent chips + results rail/grid); Player chrome + exhausted slate (text column ≤1150, kept from 1.9.2); Settings (grouped rows; the THEME SWITCHER is the Giao diện row); EXIT DIALOG (UI-1's spec: platform .alert, "Thoát Joe TV?", "Ở lại" = cancel/default focus, "Thoát" = destructive → flush → exit(0); handler inventory 7→8 re-gate owed by Team 4/UI-3).
- **THEME = LAYOUT + TOKENS (restructure order):** switching theme in Settings switches the view structure as well as colours/type/radius. Engineering shape this implies (for the build round, stated now so the pick is informed): shared data layer untouched (stores, providers, WatchStore, image pipeline are theme-blind); per-theme VIEW TREES for the 6 surfaces (Home/Grid/Detail/Search/Player/Settings chrome) selected by one @AppStorage theme id at the root; shared leaf components (PosterCard, chips, fields, buttons) parameterised by a ThemeTokens struct so a theme is (tokens + which layout branches) — the 10 below are built from 5 nav/hero layout branches, so the marginal cost of themes 4–10 is tokens + branch selection, not 10 apps. Estimated build shape: 1 theme = the redesign build; each additional theme ≈ its layout branch + token table + gate pass.
- **Convergence law compliance (all themes):** focus enters rendering only as VALUES (stroke width/colour, title colour, fill stop) inside always-installed single-shape backgrounds; no theme uses in-label scaling, focusEffectDisabled, or focus-conditional structure; the preview panel in theme 8 swaps CONTENT (data binding in an always-installed panel), not structure. Fields light in every theme (the field token is shared; only its focus-ring colour follows the theme).
- **Distinctness from Đá Banh TV:** even theme 1 keeps Joe TV's silhouette — pure-black foundation (Đá Banh = teal gradient), JOE TV wordmark with play-triangle O, top pill bar + 2:3 poster rails (Đá Banh = crest face-off hero + landscape match tiles).

### PART 4 — The 10 themes (each: 7 renders in ui-redesign/themes/<slug>/ — home, grid, detail, search, player, settings, dialog)
**1. theme-01-dabanh-green — "Xanh Đá Banh".** Concept: UI-1's completed design system, realised verbatim (its 5 seed mockups extended with grid + dialog). Survey origin: Đá Banh TV itself + Apple TV app restraint. Layout deltas vs current app: NONE in geometry (deliberate — the known map stays); the changes are construction (card anatomy v2: text inside the painted face, one r=22 shape both states), light-by-design fields, dialog added. Tokens: bg #000; surface white .13→.05 gradient; accent #0D5C4D (primary stop #0E7A64); focus #00E5FF ring 4; heroMeta #BFF0E6; radius 22/18, art inset 12 (art r=10). Dialog: scrim black .55; card = the r22 surface; "Ở lại" = green primary + cyan ring (focused), "Thoát" = quiet surface. Strengths: (a) the owner's own ordered direction, zero learning curve; (b) every construction already desk-proven against Đá Banh's device record, contrast ≈12:1 focused; (c) cheapest to build + gate (UI-1 spec is written). Weaknesses: least visually new — if he wants the redesign to FEEL new, this is the conservative pick; shares its accent family with Đá Banh (distinctness rests on layout/branding, as specced). Tech-gate note: [UI-3 to stamp]
**2. theme-02-cinema-crimson — "Điện Ảnh".** Concept: the big-screen streaming home — full-bleed backdrop hero, quiet rails. Survey origin: Netflix billboard + Max detail. Layout deltas: Home gains a 640px backdrop HERO (featured film's real 780×438 thumb, scrim to bg) with pills floating over it — the current app has no hero; Detail becomes backdrop-led (430px band, poster overlapping). Tokens: bg #0A0A0A; surfaces #232323→#171717; accent crimson #D9232E (selected stop #B3122E); focus = WHITE ring 4 + white bold title; radius 10/10 (sharp, editorial); cards 240 art. Dialog: dark card r10, "Ở lại" crimson + white ring. Strengths: (a) most cinematic first impression — the catalogue looks like a cinema, not a file list; (b) white focus is the highest-luminance ring available, impossible to sink on dark; (c) hero uses the thumb asset every source already ships. Weaknesses: hero costs the first rail's above-the-fold slot (one less rail visible); backdrop quality is capped by the 780×438 thumb (soft on 4K panels — labelled); crimson is a strong flavour some may tire of. Tech-gate note: [UI-3 to stamp]
**3. theme-03-ivory-shelf — "Kệ Sáng".** Concept: the library shelf — light, calm, organised. Survey origin: Plex/Jellyfin sidebar IA + Apple light surfaces. Layout deltas: LEFT SIDEBAR (320px) replaces the top pill bar (wordmark + vertical sections incl. Tìm phim); content shifts right; grid becomes the primary browsing surface (5 per row in the narrower column). Tokens: bg warm ivory #F4F2ED; surfaces white; text ink #14161A; accent blue #2456C8; focus = blue ring 4 + blue title; radius 16/14; fields stay light (white) — in this theme the field is native to the foundation. Dialog: white card r16 on dark scrim, "Ở lại" blue primary + ring. Strengths: (a) the only light theme — unbeatable daytime/bright-room legibility, text contrast ~15:1 class everywhere; (b) sidebar makes the 4-section map permanently visible (fewest remote presses to switch sections); (c) feels like a different app from Đá Banh at a glance. Weaknesses: light UI in a dark living room can glare at night (the owner's main viewing context — labelled honestly); poster colours pop less on white; sidebar eats 320px of rail width. Tech-gate note: [UI-3 to stamp]
**4. theme-04-mono-lime — "Tối Giản".** Concept: almost no chrome — artwork and type only, one electric accent. Survey origin: Infuse minimalism. Layout deltas: pills shrink to TEXT TABS (selected = accent underline, a value change); no hero; rails get larger gaps (34) and quieter card faces (white .06, no gradient); search tab joins the tab row. Tokens: bg #000; accent + focus = lime #A3E635 (dark text #0C1206 on accent fills); meta #9CA3AF; radius 12/12. Dialog: near-black card r12, "Ở lại" lime fill + dark text + lime ring. Strengths: (a) the calmest screen of the ten — nothing competes with the posters; (b) lime on black ≈ high contrast and never confused with any other app's accent in the house; (c) thinnest chrome = most rail area per screen. Weaknesses: underline-selected tab is subtler than a filled pill (selected state reads weaker at 3 m than themes 1/7 — labelled); lime is polarising; minimalism shows any future sloppy screen immediately. Tech-gate note: [UI-3 to stamp]
**5. theme-05-glass-ice — "Kính Băng".** Concept: frosted premium — the featured backdrop washes the whole screen under glass panels. Survey origin: tvOS 26 material language (baked, NOT live blur — the house performance law bans live blur; panels are pre-composited translucency over the static backdrop wash). Layout deltas: Home backdrop goes FULL-SCREEN ambient (bg wash at ~72% over the thumb) with the hero text block on top and rails below; top pills float as glass capsules; Detail backdrop-led like theme 2 but softer (r26). Tokens: bg #05080A; surfaces white .17→.07 gradient + bright stroke .28; accent deep ice #1280A6 (stop #0E5A74); focus #A5F3FC ring 4; radius 26/20 (softest scale). Dialog: glass card r26, "Ở lại" ice primary + pale ring. Strengths: (a) the most "2026 flagship" feel of the set; (b) ambient backdrop makes Home feel alive without any motion; (c) brightest focus ring of the dark themes. Weaknesses: translucency over changing backdrops makes text contrast backdrop-dependent (mitigated by the 72% wash + scrim, but it is the least predictable contrast in the set — labelled); if the tech gate judges baked-glass risky to reproduce in SwiftUI values, this is the theme most likely to drift from its render. Tech-gate note: [UI-3 to stamp]
**6. theme-06-warm-cinema — "Rạp Ấm".** Concept: the arthouse one-sheet — serif type, brass, numbered rails. Survey origin: Criterion-style editorial + warm cinema palettes. Layout deltas: rail titles numbered ("01 — Mới cập nhật"); Detail is CENTRED (poster centred, title/meta/synopsis centred, buttons centred) — the only centred detail in the set; servers/episodes return to left-aligned rows below. Tokens: bg warm charcoal #0B0906; surfaces #262017→#171310; accent brass #C9A227 (selected stop #8A6D1F, dark text); focus #E8C766; text warm paper #F5EDDC; radius 14/12; headings in serif. Dialog: warm card r14, "Ở lại" brass + amber ring. Strengths: (a) unmistakable personality — nothing else on his TV looks like it; (b) serif display titles make long Vietnamese titles read like film posters, not file names; (c) warm palette flatters the catalogue's many period/costume dramas. Weaknesses: serif at small meta sizes is weaker (meta stays sans — noted in the render); brass-on-dark contrast (≈7:1 class) is good but below themes 1/7; the centred detail is a bigger build delta than the shared split detail. Tech-gate note: [UI-3 to stamp]
**7. theme-07-hi-contrast — "Rõ Nét".** Concept: legibility is the design — yellow on black, bigger everything, zero translucency. Survey origin: accessibility-first TV patterns (platform guidance: focus unmistakable at distance). Layout deltas: cards grow (264 art, 5 per row), type +2pt across card/rail/settings, all surfaces OPAQUE (no gradients, no alpha faces), badges become solid yellow/black; settings rows taller. Tokens: bg #000; surface #1A1A1A flat + white .38 stroke; accent + focus #FFD60A (black text on fills); ring 6pt; radius 18/16. Dialog: opaque #1A1A1A card r18, "Ở lại" yellow fill black text + 6pt yellow ring, "Thoát" outlined. Strengths: (a) the direct, maximal answer to his repeated "chìm / không rõ" verdicts — nothing in this theme can sink, on any panel; (b) best theme for tired eyes / bright room / sitting far; (c) opaque surfaces are the cheapest to render (performance headroom). Weaknesses: least "premium" looking — deliberately utilitarian; yellow is loud over long sessions; fewer cards per row = more scrolling. Tech-gate note: [UI-3 to stamp]
**8. theme-08-midnight-sky — "Nửa Đêm".** Concept: the preview browser — a live info panel beside the rails, deep-blue night foundation. Survey origin: Apple TV app preview behaviour + Max's blue. Layout deltas: Home splits — LEFT panel (560px, always installed) shows the focused film's full meta + synopsis + actions; RIGHT column carries two rails of 4; focus stays in the rails, the panel follows (content swap = data binding, structure fixed). Tokens: bg deep blue #050D1F (a deliberate colour, not a fake black); surfaces #12264D→#0B1830; accent #2196F3 (stop #0B5FA5); focus #38BDF8 ring + title #7DD3FC; radius 20/16; cards 232 art. Dialog: blue card r20, "Ở lại" sky primary + ring. Strengths: (a) fewest presses to a decision — synopsis and Xem ngay are visible before opening Detail; (b) the panel makes Home feel rich without a hero image dependency (works even when a backdrop is missing); (c) blue family clearly distinct from Đá Banh's teal-green. Weaknesses: rails narrow to 4 per row (more horizontal scrolling); the panel is the most complex Home in the set to build and gate (focus-follows-panel must stay value-only); blue foundations are exactly what his panel amplified in v1.8 — here blue is intentional and uniform, but the association is labelled for the owner to judge. Tech-gate note: [UI-3 to stamp]
**9. theme-09-paper-ink — "Giấy & Mực".** Concept: the film pages of a newspaper — masthead, hairlines, serif, one vermilion accent. Survey origin: editorial print design translated to TV. Layout deltas: MASTHEAD home (centred wordmark + dateline rule above the pill row); rail titles in serif with the accent reserved for the focused title (focus RING is paper-white; the vermilion appears on the focused card's title); radius scale drops to 4 (near-print square) across cards/fields/dialog. Tokens: bg #111110; surfaces #1D1D1B flat; text paper #F5F1E8; accent vermilion #E4572E (stop #C7431F); focus ring #F5F1E8 4pt; meta #A79F8D. Dialog: paper-dark card r4, "Ở lại" vermilion + paper ring. Strengths: (a) the most editorially distinct — reads as a curated film journal, suits a hand-picked catalogue; (b) hairline rules give rails a structure that survives even before posters load (the placeholder state looks intentional); (c) near-square corners are the sharpest silhouette of the set — zero corner-residue ambiguity. Weaknesses: print language can read "dry" next to streaming apps; serif + small caps dateline is a taste bet; white focus ring on paper-white text is lower chroma contrast than themes 1/7 ( luminance contrast still high — labelled). Tech-gate note: [UI-3 to stamp]
**10. theme-10-graphite-orange — "Graphite".** Concept: the power browser — chunky graphite surfaces, signal orange, poster-led hero. Survey origin: Plex's home + Android TV density. Layout deltas: Home hero is a SPLIT POSTER hero (the 2:3 poster at 264×396 in a card surface + full info right) instead of a backdrop hero — it spends the screen on the asset class that is actually high-resolution; cards grow to 256 art with 32 gaps. Tokens: bg #0C0C0C; surfaces #262626→#171717; accent orange #F97316 (stop #C2570B, dark text); focus #FB923C; radius 16/14. Dialog: graphite card r16, "Ở lại" orange + ring. Strengths: (a) hero quality is never capped by the small 780×438 thumb — the poster asset is 500×750+; (b) orange-on-graphite is a proven, friendly, high-energy pairing far from both violet and teal; (c) bigger cards = bigger focus targets, friendlier D-pad landings. Weaknesses: split hero shows less "cinema" than a backdrop hero; orange + FHD badges + red progress bars are three warm accents to keep disciplined (progress stays red by semantics — noted); cards at 256 art show 5 per row + a peek. Tech-gate note: [UI-3 to stamp]

### PART 5 — Comparison + recommended top 3
| # | Theme | Foundation | Accent / Focus | Nav | Hero | Card art | Radius | Type voice | Best for | Main cost |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Xanh Đá Banh | pure black | #0D5C4D / cyan ring | pills | none | 240 | 22 | sans | owner's ordered direction, lowest risk | least visually new |
| 2 | Điện Ảnh | #0A0A0A | crimson / white ring | pills | backdrop 640 | 240 | 10 | sans bold | cinema feel | hero eats a rail slot; thumb-capped backdrop |
| 3 | Kệ Sáng | ivory light | blue / blue ring | sidebar | none | 232 | 16 | sans | daytime legibility | night glare; narrower rails |
| 4 | Tối Giản | black | lime / lime ring | text tabs | none | 240 | 12 | sans light | calm, chrome-free | weaker selected state |
| 5 | Kính Băng | near-black teal | ice / pale-cyan ring | pills | ambient full | 240 | 26 | sans | flagship feel | contrast varies with backdrop |
| 6 | Rạp Ấm | warm charcoal | brass / amber ring | pills | none | 240 | 14 | serif display | personality, period catalogue | centred detail = extra build |
| 7 | Rõ Nét | black opaque | yellow / yellow 6pt | pills | none | 264 | 18 | sans +2pt | never-sunk legibility | utilitarian look; 5/row |
| 8 | Nửa Đêm | deep blue | sky / sky ring | pills | preview panel | 232 | 20 | sans | fastest pick-a-film | complex home; 4/row |
| 9 | Giấy & Mực | #111110 | vermilion / paper ring | masthead+pills | none | 240 | 4 | serif print | curated-journal feel | taste bet; dry next to streamers |
| 10 | Graphite | graphite | orange / orange ring | pills | split poster | 256 | 16 | sans | poster-quality hero | less cinema; warm-accent discipline |
**UI-2's recommended top 3 (owner picks the final 3):** (1) **Theme 1 Xanh Đá Banh** — his own ordered system, fully specced by UI-1, the safe default that must be in the build set; (2) **Theme 2 Điện Ảnh** — the strongest felt upgrade for a movie app (the hero is what every surveyed flagship leads with) while staying in the proven dark family and reusing UI-1's constructions unchanged; (3) **Theme 7 Rõ Nét** — the insurance policy for his core complaint: whichever other two he picks, one built theme guarantees the nothing-ever-sinks experience, and its opaque/cheap rendering is the performance anchor of the set. Runner-up: theme 10 (if he prefers the poster-led hero to the backdrop hero, swap it for theme 2).

### Honesty declaration
- **Claims:** survey patterns per app (Part 1); KK field presence on 48/48 sampled items + featured-film detail values (Part 2); title-length distribution (min 8 / median 20 / mean 23.3 / max 71 chars, same 48 items); every theme's renders exist as HTML files generated from one content set (file list in the handoff); construction compliance of the render language with UI-1's rules (by construction of the generator's CSS: single-shape surfaces, ring/title value changes only, light fields, no scale transforms anywhere in the generated CSS).
- **Evidence:** live API responses fetched this session (phimapi.com newest pages 1–2 + the featured film's detail call); UI-1's Part A/B measurements and TVPalette values read from DaBanhTV source; web survey results (Apple HIG tvOS guidance, Netflix/Plex/Infuse/Jellyfin/Stremio pattern notes — sources cited inline in Part 1 by app, retrieved via web search this session).
- **Unverified, labelled:** (i) NO pixel rendering was possible — no PNG screenshots exist (renderer situation identical to UI-1's finding); the HTML renders are faithful to the token/layout spec but the desk cannot see them composited, and the leader presents them as files to open at 1920×1080; (ii) survey claims about current shipping app UIs rest on web sources + written pattern notes, not on hands-on captures of those apps this session; (iii) theme 8's panel-follows-focus and theme 5's baked-glass contrast are design-level judgements awaiting the UI-3 technical gate and, ultimately, device rendering; (iv) per-theme build-cost shape in Part 3 is an engineering estimate for the pick discussion, not a quote.

## TEAM UI-3 — TECHNICAL GATE OF THE 10 THEMES (2026-11-15)

Scope: verdicts + cost notes ONLY. No design changes, no app code edited. Method: every theme was gated from PRIMARY evidence — the generator (`ui-redesign/themes/gen.py`, the single construction source for all 70 renders) plus per-theme greps of the generated HTML — not from the theme cards' self-descriptions. Contrast figures below are computed from the token hex values (WCAG relative-luminance formula) in this session.

### Pack-wide findings (apply to all 10, verified once, not repeated per theme)
- **Focus construction is uniform and law-clean.** In the generator's shared CSS, `.focused` changes ONLY `box-shadow` (inset ring, width/colour values) and title `color` — for cards, pills, chips, buttons, settings rows, sidebar items alike. Card geometry is defined by a single `.card` rule; the focused state cannot change size, position, or silhouette. Grep over ALL 70 generated HTML files + index: **zero** `scale(`, `transition`, `animation`, `filter:`, `backdrop-filter` — the only `transform` in the pack is the dialog's centring `translate(-50%,-50%)` (positioning, state-independent). No theme needs live blur; every surface is a static fill/gradient/stroke.
- **Text fitting vs the measured data.** `.card .title` is `nowrap + overflow:hidden + text-overflow:ellipsis` INSIDE the painted face — the 45-char proof title ("Hiệp Sĩ Mặt Nạ MY-TH: Huyền Thoại 12 Con Giáp") ships in the shared content set, and the measured 71-char max ellipsises identically (ellipsis is length-independent). `.card .meta` is `nowrap` without ellipsis, but its content is bounded by the data ("2026 · Tập 229" ≈ 15 chars ≈ 190px at 23pt < the narrowest art width, 232px). Detail titles are unclamped; a 71-char title wraps to 3 lines at 64pt and the detail page still fits its 1080 budget (estimate, ~140px headroom) in every theme except where noted (theme 8's panel).
- **Exit dialog (criterion 5):** grep-verified in all 10 `dialog.html` — "Ở lại" is `.btn.primary.focused`, "Thoát" is the plain `.btn`; the dialog is one card shape on a .55 scrim. Per UI-1's spec the SHIPPED dialog is the platform `.alert` (system chrome, cancel-role default focus); the HTML is the visual target if ever hand-drawn. All 10 honour the shape.
- **Search field (criterion 5):** all 10 `search.html` carry exactly one `.field.focused` built from the SHARED light tokens (BASE: #F5F5F7 fill / #141416 text / #6E6E73 placeholder) — UI-1's light-by-design rule holds in every theme; only the ring colour follows the theme. Settings token fields use the same `.field` construction.
- **Switchability (criterion 4):** the pack itself is the proof of shape — one generator = token table + 4 nav branches (pills/tabs/sidebar/masthead) + 5 hero branches (none/backdrop/ambient/preview/split) + theme 6's centred-detail branch, over one content set. No theme references a field outside UI-2 Part 2's measured set (poster/thumb, year, FHD quality, episode labels, genres, actors, synopsis — all exist on the sources).
- **Performance (criterion 3):** nothing in any theme animates or re-renders per frame. Backdrop-class assets are the measured thumbs (780×438 ≈ 1.4 MB decoded); the one measured 3840×2160 thumb outlier (519 KB) must ride UI-1's downscale-to-≤1920 decode path (≈8.3 MB bitmap) — this binds themes 2 and 5. All themes are compatible with UI-1's pipeline (persistent cache, downscale-at-decode, rail prefetch) as specced.
- **Render-scope note (not a defect):** home.html/grid.html are scrolling surfaces; the fixed 1080px HTML clips below-fold rows by construction (`overflow:hidden`). In the app these scroll vertically. Recorded once so the presentation is not misread as clipping.

### Per-theme verdicts (the stamp of record for each card's `[UI-3 to stamp]` line)
**1. theme-01-dabanh-green "Xanh Đá Banh" — PASS.** UI-1's system verbatim: focus cyan #00E5FF on black = 13.7:1 (computed); one r22 shape both states; light fields; dialog/search conform (pack-wide findings). No notes.
**2. theme-02-cinema-crimson "Điện Ảnh" — PASS-WITH-COST-NOTE.** Focus model clean (white ring 4pt + white title; white on the crimson pill = 6.9:1). COST NOTE: hero and detail header text sit over the backdrop thumb with gradient scrims that fade to fully transparent INSIDE the text block — in `home.html` the 90° scrim is solid only to 22% of 1920px and reaches transparent at 65%, while the synopsis block extends to ~51% width, where the scrim alpha is ≈0.33 → worst-case white-text contrast over a hypothetical white backdrop ≈ **1.45:1** at line tails (the rendered backdrop-01 is dark, so the render itself reads fine; the FEATURED slot rotates real thumbs, so the bound is the design's, not the sample's). A faithful build must add a scrim floor across the full text zone (a static fill/token change — the design survives it). Second note: the hero consumes `thumb_url`; the featured slot must be restricted to thumb-bearing sources (or fall back to the poster), since Torrent/Cinemeta metadata is poster-only per Part 2.
**3. theme-03-ivory-shelf "Kệ Sáng" — PASS.** Sidebar geometry verified in the generated CSS (320px sidebar, content margin 280 inside the padded screen — no overlap); grid generator math gives 5 cards/row in the narrowed column (cardW 252, gap 26, avail 1480). Blue focus on white = 6.5:1; text ink on white ~15:1 class. The night-glare weakness is a design trade-off already on the card, not a technical cost. No notes.
**4. theme-04-mono-lime "Tối Giản" — PASS-WITH-COST-NOTE.** Cards/buttons/chips keep the pack-standard 4px ring. COST NOTE: the nav TAB focus is the pack's only ring-less focus site — `.tab.focused` in the generated CSS is colour + weight only (`color:#A3E635;font-weight:700`, grep-verified in theme-04/home.html). It does NOT fail the confusability test — lime on black computes to 13.9:1, the second-highest focus contrast in the pack — but focus there is carried by colour/weight alone, and the presentation should say so; if the owner picks this theme, adding the same inset ring token to tabs is a one-line build decision, not a redesign.
**5. theme-05-glass-ice "Kính Băng" — PASS-WITH-COST-NOTE (hard note; UI-2's own risk flag confirmed and quantified).** Blur: the render uses NO blur and needs none — the "glass" is baked translucency (surface gradient white .17→.07, stroke .28) over a static ambient wash; every element is a static fill, so the performance constitution is honoured and the look is implementable in SwiftUI values. COST NOTE (contrast floor): EVERY text-bearing surface sits over the ambient image with only the `${bg}B8` wash (alpha 0xB8 ≈ 0.72, grep-verified in theme-05/home.html) between text and imagery. Worst-case bound, computed: a white backdrop composited under the wash gives L≈0.28 → white text ≈ **3.2:1**; the dialog (scrim .55 + the same translucent surface) computes to the same ≈3.2:1, with its meta text (white .66) lower still. Typical KK thumbs are dark and will read far better, but the guarantee is the bound, not the typical. A faithful build must raise the wash behind text-bearing zones to a guaranteed floor (≥85%) or give card text blocks an opaque backing — either makes the shipped theme read somewhat less airy than this render. Plus theme 2's thumb-dependency note for the ambient hero.
**6. theme-06-warm-cinema "Rạp Ấm" — PASS.** The centred detail exists as rendered (slug branch in the generator; `text-align:center` verified in theme-06/detail.html) and is a layout branch within the pack's switchability shape (its extra build cost is already stated by UI-2). Brass focus #E8C766 on the card surface = 11.3:1 (computed); serif is display-only, meta stays sans per the render. No notes.
**7. theme-07-hi-contrast "Rõ Nét" — PASS.** The pack's legibility/performance anchor, verified: 6px ring, yellow #FFD60A on black = 14.9:1 (computed, highest in the pack), fully opaque surfaces, grid math 5/row at cardW 288 (grep-verified). No notes.
**8. theme-08-midnight-sky "Nửa Đêm" — PASS-WITH-COST-NOTE (UI-2's second risk flag — examined hardest, confirmed in one specific).** The panel mechanism itself is CLEAN: the 560px panel is always installed (verified in theme-08/home.html), focus stays in the rails with the standard ring+title values, and the panel swap is content binding, not structure — convergence law intact. COST NOTE (no line budget): the generator encodes NO clamp for the panel (grep: `line-clamp` count 0 in gen.py and theme-08/home.html). The render's sample synopsis is ≈400 chars and fits (~800px panel vs ~930px available); the MEASURED real synopsis is ~700 chars (UI-2 Part 2), which at 24pt in the panel's 488px inner width runs ≈18 lines ≈ 648px, pushing the panel to ≈994px — past the ~930px available beside the top bar — and a 71-char title (≈3 lines at 52pt in-panel) adds ≈124px more. A faithful build must clamp the panel (title ≤2 lines, synopsis ≤8 lines + ellipsis): a spec/token addition that does not change the layout's identity, but the render as drawn does not contain it.
**9. theme-09-paper-ink "Giấy & Mực" — PASS.** Focus is never colour-alone: the ring is paper-white #F5F1E8 on the flat #1D1D1B surface (high luminance contrast), and the vermilion focused title computes to 4.6:1 at 25pt semibold — adequate as the secondary carrier. r4 corners are a single consistent shape in both states. No notes.
**10. theme-10-graphite-orange "Graphite" — PASS.** Split hero spends the screen on the poster asset (500×750 class, always present — no thumb dependency); orange focus #FB923C on #0C0C0C = 8.6:1 (computed); grid math 5/row at cardW 280 (grep-verified). No notes.

- **Cleared to enter the owner's presentation UNCHANGED (6):** themes 1, 3, 6, 7, 9, 10.
- **Cleared to enter WITH COST NOTES (4)** — the note that must travel with each theme, quoted:
  - Theme 2: "Hero/detail text sits over the backdrop with scrims that fade to transparent inside the text block (worst-case ≈1.45:1 at synopsis line tails over a white backdrop); the build must add a scrim floor across the text zone. The hero also requires a landscape thumb — sources without one (Torrent) cannot feed it."
  - Theme 4: "Nav-tab focus is colour + weight only (no ring) — the highest-contrast hue in the pack (13.9:1) but the only ring-less focus site; cards/buttons keep the standard ring."
  - Theme 5: "No live blur is used or needed, but all text sits over the ambient image behind only a 72% wash — worst-case white-text contrast ≈3.2:1 on a bright backdrop; the build must raise the wash behind text zones to a guaranteed floor, which will read slightly less airy than the render."

Themes 5 and 8 — the two UI-2 flagged itself — were examined hardest and both survive as cost notes, not failures: theme 5's risk is a bounded contrast floor (fixable by a token), theme 8's is a missing clamp (fixable by a spec line); neither violates the focus law, the data reality in its rendered state, or the performance budget.

### Honesty declaration
- **Claims:** the pack-wide construction facts (grep results over gen.py + all 70 generated HTML files, cited inline); the per-theme geometry/token facts (each grep-verified in that theme's own files); the contrast figures (computed from token hex values with the WCAG luminance formula, this session); the theme-8 panel overflow estimate (arithmetic from the generator's own dimensions + UI-2's measured 700-char synopsis).
- **Evidence:** `ui-redesign/themes/gen.py` read in full; greps for forbidden effects (0 hits), dialog "Ở lại" primary+focused (10/10), search `.field.focused` (10/10), theme-5 wash token `#05080AB8`, theme-8 panel rule + `line-clamp` absence (0), theme-4 `.tab.focused` rule, theme-3/7/10 card widths, theme-6 centred-detail branch.
- **Unverified, labelled:** (i) NO pixel rendering was possible (same VM renderer limitation UI-1/UI-2 recorded) — all contrast/geometry claims are computed from the CSS/token values, not observed on a composited screen, and tvOS's own .card platter behaviour under these constructions remains a device question for the build round; (ii) worst-case contrast bounds assume a hypothetical pure-white backdrop/thumb — the real catalogue's thumbs are typically dark, so field contrast will usually be far better than the bounds quoted; the bounds are the guarantee the design can make, which is what a gate must stamp; (iii) the theme-8 panel-height figure is an estimate from font metrics (chars/line at 24pt in 488px), not a rendered measurement — its direction (overflow at real data lengths) is robust to the estimate's error bars, its exact pixel count is not claimed.

## TEAM UI-4 — SIX STRUCTURAL DESIGNS (2026-11-15)

Scope: REDO of the rejected UI-2 pack under the owner's verdict (colours kept, structure was the failure). Six designs, each a different APP STRUCTURE anchored to a different surveyed app's real structure — NOT six token sets on one skeleton. Files: `JoeTV/ui-redesign/themes2/design-A|B|C|D|E|F/` × the same 7 screens (home, grid, detail, search, player, settings, dialog) = 42 HTML files at 1920×1080 + `themes2/index.html`; generator `themes2/gen2.py`. SAME content set as UI-2 verbatim (featured "Yêu Là Tàn Nhẫn" + the same 12 films/posters + query "tình" + the 1.9.2 slate copy + the 1.9.2 settings rows), copied from UI-2's gen.py constants. Posters referenced as `../../posters/` from each design folder. No app code edited. UI-1's construction rules bind throughout (verified by grep, see honesty declaration): one painted shape per control, focus = value-only changes, no scale, no silhouette change, no live blur, fields light by design, exit dialog per UI-1's spec in every design ("Ở lại" primary + focused, 6/6 grep-verified).

**Colour layer (owner's 15:14 CST update — pre-approved palettes applied as ordered):** A = old theme 2 crimson system verbatim (#D9232E/#B3122E, white focus ring, r10); F = UI-1 / old theme 1 Đá Banh green verbatim (#0D5C4D, focus #00E5FF, r22); D = old theme 7 hi-contrast verbatim (#FFD60A on black, fully opaque, 6pt ring, +2pt type) adapted to the poster-wall anatomy; B = graphite-orange (old theme 10 family); C = deep blue (old theme 8 family); E = ivory paper & ink with vermilion, serif display.

### DESIGN A — "Billboard" (Netflix structure) · crimson
- **Fingerprint:** (1) Nav: top bar whose pills float OVER the hero (translucent dark fills), solid on non-hero screens. (2) Home: full-bleed backdrop billboard (featured thumb, 668px) + poster rails below. (3) Card: inside-face card (UI-1 anatomy), art 240 + FHD badge + title + meta inside the face, r10. (4) Detail: backdrop-led — 430px backdrop band, poster frame overlapping the band's lower edge, info right. (5) Search: centred light field over a poster results rail. (6) Player: centred slate column (left-aligned text, max-width 1150), no other chrome.
- **Per-screen notes:** Home hero text zone carries a strengthened scrim (90° gradient .94 → .62 → .12 across the text block, plus bottom fade) — UI-3's theme-2 note partially answered in the render; synopsis pre-truncated to a 3-line budget (~205 chars at the hero's width). Grid = chips + 6×2 inside-face cards. Dialog over the billboard home.
- **Weaknesses (honest):** the build must still guarantee a scrim FLOOR under hero/detail text for bright thumbs (the render's gradient is a design value, not a proof for every thumb); the hero consumes `thumb_url`, so poster-only sources (Torrent/Cinemeta) cannot feed the featured slot; the billboard pushes rail 1 down — Home's first screen is one film deep (Netflix's own trade-off, taken knowingly).
- **Build shape:** hero header component + the standard rail/detail/search components; backdrop rides UI-1's pipeline (thumb decoded ≤1920 wide). Closest of the six to a conventional streaming-app build.

### DESIGN B — "Sidebar Library" (Plex structure) · graphite-orange
- **Fingerprint:** (1) Nav: PERMANENT left sidebar (320px) on every screen except the player. (2) Home: rails only, in the narrowed column — no hero anywhere in the design. (3) Card: inside-face card, art 232 + badge + title + meta (5 per rail in the column). (4) Detail: split — poster left, info centre, and a RIGHT COLUMN of lists (8 episode rows + 5 source rows). (5) Search: sidebar stays (Tìm phim selected); light field + poster grid inside the column. (6) Player: slate PLUS a bottom transport bar (progress track, time, source chip) — the only design with player chrome beyond the slate.
- **Per-screen notes:** Grid is the primary browse surface (5+5+2 in the column, filter chips). Detail's right column ≈ 906px of the ~974px available — fits as rendered. Settings rows live in the column at full column width.
- **Weaknesses (honest):** the sidebar permanently costs 320px (5 cards/row instead of 6, more horizontal scrolling); the detail list column is the densest screen in the pack — a future longer source list overflows it first; graphite-orange is the least "cinema" of the dark designs.
- **Build shape:** one root shell fork (sidebar) shared by all B screens; the detail needs the two-list column component; everything else is standard components at narrower widths.

### DESIGN C — "Preview Panel" (Apple TV app behaviour; old theme 8 developed) · deep blue
- **Fingerprint:** (1) Nav: top pill bar on the solid foundation. (2) Home: always-installed info PANEL left (560px) that follows focus + two rails of 4 right. (3) Card: LEAN inside-face card — art + title only; the FHD badge and meta line are RELOCATED to the panel (the panel is where metadata lives in this design). (4) Detail: grows out of the panel — panel left (poster + title + meta + genres + synopsis + actions), source chips + episode grid right. (5) Search: search panel left (field + recents + result count) + result LIST ROWS right (poster thumb + title + meta + FHD badge) — no poster grid. (6) Player: slate text beside a poster panel.
- **Per-screen notes:** UI-3's theme-8 cost note is APPLIED, not carried: panel title ≤ 2 lines, panel synopsis ≤ 8 lines + ellipsis (pre-truncated in the renders at ~330 chars for the panel's 488px inner width; detail panel ~300). Grid keeps a mini-panel (400px) beside a 4-wide grid. The 45-char ellipsis-proof title sits in a C card on the grid screen.
- **Weaknesses (honest):** rails narrow to 4 per row; the panel is the most complex Home in the pack to build and gate, and its clamps must be implemented as real layout constraints in the build, not text truncation; the deep-blue foundation carries the association with what the owner's panel amplified in v1.8 (intentional and uniform here — labelled for him to judge, again).
- **Build shape:** one panel component bound to the focus selection (content binding only — the mechanism UI-3 already cleared on theme 8); one result-row component for search; detail reuses the panel.

### DESIGN D — "Poster Wall" (Infuse/Jellyfin structure) · hi-contrast yellow/black
- **Fingerprint:** (1) Nav: minimal text-link top bar (no pills, no sidebar; selected = yellow underline value). (2) Home: NO hero, NO rails — Home IS a full poster wall (filter chips + 6-wide grid). (3) Card: POSTER-ONLY art block (264×396, FHD badge on art); title + meta sit BELOW the art — the one design with this anatomy, as briefed. (4) Detail: centred poster page (poster, title, meta, chips, synopsis, actions — all centred). (5) Search: light field + results wall. (6) Player: centred hi-vis slate (centred text, yellow primary).
- **Contrast guarantee for the below-art text (stated as required):** the text never sits on imagery or on any surface that can lighten — it sits directly on the app's own opaque pure-black foundation (#000, the design's page background, painted by the app). White title on #000 = 21:1; meta #E5E5E5 on #000 ≈ 16:1; focused title/ring #FFD60A on #000 ≈ 14.9:1 (UI-3's computed figure for the same pairing). Focus = 6pt yellow ring ON THE ART + title → yellow: value changes only, geometry identical in both states.
- **Weaknesses (honest):** below-art text makes each film taller (the wall's second row sits near the fold — fewer films per screen than inside-face designs at equal art size); with no hero, Home's first impression is exactly as good as the catalogue's posters that day; yellow is loud over long sessions (owner-kept palette, stated plainly); the uncached placeholder is a plain dark art block (title/meta still present below it, by design).
- **Build shape:** the simplest components in the pack (art block + two text lines; no card surface exists); Home and Grid are one screen shape; fully opaque surfaces = the cheapest rendering of the six.

### DESIGN E — "Magazine" (editorial structure) · ivory paper & ink
- **Fingerprint:** (1) Nav: masthead — centred wordmark, double rule, small-caps text nav line. (2) Home: featured STORY block (framed poster + headline + byline + synopsis) + numbered INDEX ROWS (01–05). (3) Card: the index row itself — number + landscape thumb (poster cropped 176×110) + serif title + meta + episode tag. (4) Detail: two-column ARTICLE — poster column (framed poster + source list under it) + text column with the synopsis FIRST, then actors/genres/actions/episode chips. (5) Search: masthead + light field + index rows. (6) Player: the slate as a bordered article card on the paper foundation.
- **Per-screen notes:** Grid = a two-column compact index of all 12 films (the table-of-contents screen). Fields stay light by design — here white on the ivory foundation, ink text, vermilion ring. Focus on rows = vermilion inset ring + title → vermilion (values only).
- **Weaknesses (honest):** a light UI in a dark living room can glare at night — the owner's main viewing context, the standing objection to light foundations; serif/print grammar is a taste bet; rows show a 110px thumb, so browsing is by title rather than by cover; the magazine home fits the story + ~3.5 rows in 1080 (the rest scroll) — the least poster-forward home of the six.
- **Build shape:** masthead shell + two row densities (home rows, index cells) + the article detail; the shared light-field token is native to this foundation.

### DESIGN F — "Source Board" (Stremio structure) · Đá Banh green (UI-1 system)
- **Fingerprint:** (1) Nav: top pill bar on pure black (UI-1 grammar verbatim). (2) Home: organised BY SOURCE — full-width source header bands (name + catalogue note + "Xem tất cả ›"), each band with its own rail. (3) Card: UI-1 PosterCard v2 (art 240 + FHD badge + title + meta inside, r22) PLUS a source badge chip on the art (KK/VS/…) — the card itself declares its source. (4) Detail: SOURCE-LIST-FIRST — compact metadata head (160px poster + title/meta/genres/actions), then five full-width source rows as the primary list (state per source: ● Đang phát / Sẵn sàng / Cần token), episode chips under, synopsis demoted to a 2-line small print. (5) Search: results GROUPED BY SOURCE (group header + rail per source). (6) Player: slate with the four attempts rendered as source rows (✗ per source) instead of the "Đã thử" line — same copy, row form.
- **Weaknesses (honest):** the structure assumes the owner thinks in sources (he curated them — but the app shows its plumbing first, by design); the same film can legitimately appear in several bands (each band is that source's own catalogue — duplication across bands is inherent); detail's 2-line synopsis is the weakest storytelling of the six; the Torrent band is empty until tokens are configured.
- **Build shape:** band-header + rail unit repeated per source; the detail's source-row component is reused by the player's attempt list; cards are UI-1's PosterCard v2 + one badge — the cheapest card build of the six.

### Honesty declaration
- **Claims:** the 42 files exist as listed (find count 43 incl. themes2/index.html; each screen file carries exactly one 1920×1080 `.screen` canvas); the content set is UI-2's verbatim (FILMS/FEATURED/SLATE constants copied from themes/gen.py, including the real fetched synopsis and the 45-char ellipsis-proof title, which appears in all 6 grid screens); construction compliance (grep over all 42 files: ZERO `scale(`, `transition`, `animation`, `filter:`, `backdrop-filter` — the only `transform` in the pack is the dialog's centring `translate(-50%,-50%)`, state-independent positioning, plus `text-transform` keywords); every dialog carries "Ở lại" as `.btn.primary.focused` (6/6); every settings screen carries its own design's Giao diện name; all poster references are `../../posters/` and resolve to existing files (11 assets, PIL-verified dimensions: posters 500×750-class with 1440×2160 outliers, backdrop 780×438); the matrix above, counted with ties against the pack.

- **Unverified, labelled:** (i) NO pixel rendering was performed (the VM renderer limitation stands; the leader renders the screenshots this round) — geometry fits are arithmetic from the generators' own dimensions, and scrolling surfaces (home/grid/search lists) clip below-fold content in the fixed 1080 frame by design, as with the previous pack; (ii) synopsis line budgets are character-count arithmetic at stated chars-per-line for each design's panel/hero widths, not rendered measurements; (iii) tvOS buildability of each structure is a design-level claim for the build round's gates — nothing here is device-proven; (iv) archetype fidelity rests on UI-2's web-sourced survey (Part 1), not on hands-on captures of the surveyed apps.
